Braided tensor product of von Neumann algebras
Kenny De Commer, Jacek Krajczok

Abstract
. We introduce a definition of braided tensor product M ⁡ ⊠ ¯ ​ N \M\overline{\boxtimes}\N of von Neumann algebras equipped with an action of a quasi-triangular quantum group 𝔾 \mathbb{G} (this includes the case when 𝔾 \mathbb{G} is a Drinfeld double). It is a new von Neumann algebra which comes together with embeddings of M , N \M,\N and the unique action of 𝔾 \mathbb{G} for which embeddings are equivariant. More generally, we construct braided tensor product of von Neumann algebras equipped with actions of locally compact quantum groups linked by a bicharacter. We study several examples, in particular we show that crossed products can be realised as braided tensor products. We also show that one can take the braided tensor product ϑ 1 ⊠ ϑ 2 \vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2} of normal, completely bounded maps which are equivariant, but this fails without the equivariance condition.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 46L67, Secondary 46L55, 46L06

1.
Introduction

Existence of fruitful connections between theories of locally compact groups and operator algebras has been well established throughout the years. In particular, one can study symmetries of a given von Neumann algebra
\M
by considering actions on it by locally compact groups, described by point-
w^{*}
-continuous homomorphisms
G\rightarrow\Aut(\M)
. For example, Connes showed in
[
6
]
that deep structural properties of
\M
are encoded in the action of
\mathbb{R}
on
\M
via modular automorphisms. As an another example, given an action
G\curvearrowright\M
, one can consider the associated crossed product von Neumann algebra
G\ltimes\M
. Algebras of this kind were among the first examples of type III factors (
[
45
]
).

A very broad generalisation of the theory of locally compact groups was introduced by Kustermans and Vaes (
[
26
,
27
]
); objects they define are called
locally compact quantum groups
. Every locally compact group gives rise to a locally compact quantum group (these are often called
classical
), and one can take the dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
of any locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
. Consequently, the theory of Kustermans and Vaes contains an extension of the classical Pontryagin duality. In particular, one can take the dual of any classical (not necesarilly abelian) locally compact group
G
. The quantum group
\widehat{G}
is described by objects studied in abstract harmonic analysis; for example
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{G})=\operatorname{vN}(G)
is the group von Neumann algebra and
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{G})=\mathrm{C}^{*}(G)
is the full group
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra.

In the realm of actions of locally compact groups, a basic construction of a new action is given by the tensor product of actions. More precisely, if we have two actions
\alpha^{\M}\colon G\curvearrowright\M,\alpha^{\N}\colon G\curvearrowright\N
, then we can consider a “diagonal” action on
\M\bar{\otimes}\N
defined via
G\ni g\mapsto\alpha^{\M}(g)\otimes\alpha^{\N}(g)\in\Aut(\M\bar{\otimes}\N)
. It is characterised by the property that the canonical embeddings
\M,\N\rightarrow\M\bar{\otimes}\N
are equivariant.

As with classical groups, one can consider actions of locally compact quantum groups on von Neumann algebras
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
. Unlike in the classical case mentioned above, it is however not necessarily true that if
\mathbb{G}
acts on
\M,\N
, then there is an action on
\M\bar{\otimes}\N
for which the canonical embeddings are equivariant. An easy counterexample is given by two copies of the translation action
\Delta_{\widehat{F_{2}}}\colon\widehat{F_{2}}\curvearrowright\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{F_{2}})=\operatorname{vN}(F_{2})
. Our work shows, that if we have actions of
\mathbb{G}
and its dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
on
\M,\N
which satisfy the
Yetter-Drinfeld
condition (Definition
2.8
), then one can define a twisted version of tensor product, called the
braided tensor product

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
(Definition
4.1
). It is a von Neumann algebra together with canonical embeddings
\M,\N\rightarrow\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
and actions
\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
such that the embeddings are equivariant (Proposition
6.3
). The same conclusion holds more generally, if one replaces actions of
\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
by an action of a quasi-triangular quantum group. This is indeed a generalisation, as pairs of actions of
\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
which satisfy the Yetter-Drinfeld condition correspond bijectively to actions of the Drinfeld double
D(\mathbb{G})
, which is a quasi-triangular quantum group.

In fact, we define the braided tensor product
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
more generally
for any two von Neumann algebras
\M,\N
equipped with actions
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
of locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
which are linked via a bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. At this level of generality however, we don’t obtain any action on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
(the quasi-triangular case corresponds to
\mathbb{H}=\mathbb{G}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
being the
\operatorname{R}
-matrix).

Let us mention that the construction of the braided tensor product is well-known and of fundamental importance in Hopf algebra theory: we refer to
[
29
,
18
]
for many instances and applications of ‘braided’ mathematics in an algebraic setting. Similar constructions have also appeared in the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebraic setting: a minimal version of the braided tensor product in
[
31
,
32
,
34
]
, and a maximal version in
[
39
]
. Our definition is close to the one of
[
31
]
(up to conventions), but because of the fact that we work with von Neumann algebras our arguments are very different. For example, one defines the braided tensor product
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
to be a
\sigma\textsc{-wot}
-closed subspace spanned by elements of the form
\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)
(analogs of simple tensors), but it is not obvious why this subspace is closed under multiplication. We reach this conclusion (after some preparations) in Theorem
4.9
. Our argument uses the biduality theorem (
[
47
, Theorem 2.6]
) to reduce the problem to the case of dual actions, where a direct calculation is possible. In the restricted setting of Yetter-Drinfeld actions of discrete quantum groups, a version of braided tensor product appeared also in
[
33
]
. A special case of our construction with
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{R},\mathbb{H}=\widehat{\mathbb{R}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
equal to the Kac-Takesaki operator appeared in the work of Houdayer on type
\operatorname{III}_{1}
factors
[
19
]
(see Example
8.3
).

\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebraic braided tensor products are used in the theory of “braided
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-quantum groups” (
[
38
,
21
,
36
]
) and we hope to use in the future our construction to study “braided locally compact quantum groups” in the setting of von Neumann algebras and weights.

Let us describe how the paper is structured. In the next section we recall the necessary parts of the theory of locally compact quantum groups and Drinfeld doubles. In Section
3
we define braided flip operators
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
which depend on the chosen bicharacter. When
\mathbb{G}
is a quasi-triangular quantum group and braided flip operators are defined using
\operatorname{R}
-matrix as the bicharacter, then
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
make
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
into a braided monoidal category (Proposition
3.4
). In Section
4
we fix locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
, a bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
, actions
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
and their implementations. Then we define
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
using braided flip operators, show that it is a von Neumann algebra (Theorem
4.9
) and that it does not depend on the chosen implementations (Proposition
4.4
). As an important technical tool, we introduce a universal lift of the action
\alpha^{\M}\colon\M\rightarrow\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\M
to a map
\alpha^{\M,u}\colon\M\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\M
with similar properties. In Section
5
we show that one can take the braided tensor product of maps
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
if they are normal, CB and equivariant (Proposition
5.1
). Then we use it to prove restricted stability of approximation properties under the braided tensor product construction (Proposition
5.3
). In the next section we assume that
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{H}
is a quasi-triangular quantum group with an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
, construct the canonical action on the braided tensor product (Proposition
6.3
) and prove associativity of
\overline{\boxtimes}
(Proposition
6.6
). In fact, we obtain more general statements by considering actions of canonical quantum subgroups
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}
(if
\mathbb{G}=D(\mathbb{H})
is the Drinfeld double, then
\mathbb{G}_{1}=\mathbb{H}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}=\widehat{\mathbb{H}}
). Section
7
is devoted to the construction of an infinite braided tensor product. It also depends on the choice of invariant states, similarly as the usual infinite tensor product. In Section
8
we describe several examples, in particular we show that the braided tensor product of (non-equivariant) functionals might fail to exist (Proposition
8.3
), it can happen that
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\nsimeq\N\overline{\boxtimes}\M
(Proposition
8.6
), and that crossed products can be realised as braided tensor products (Proposition
8.4
). This allows us to easily conclude that the braided tensor product behaves differently than the usual tensor product when considering types,
w^{*}
CBAP or factoriality of the involved von Neumann algebras. The Appendix contains two lemmas concerning locally compact quantum groups.

We write
\chi
for the flip map on tensor product of algebras and
\Sigma
for the flip of Hilbert spaces. Symbol
\overline{\lin}^{\,\tau}
will denote the closure of the linear span in topology
\tau
, while
\overline{\lin}
means the norm-closure. Throughout the paper we use leg numbering notation (with flips), so e.g.
x_{[32]}=\mathbbm{1}\otimes\chi(x)
. For a von Neumann algebra,
\CB^{\sigma}(\M)
means the space of completely bounded, normal maps equipped with the CB norm. Whenever
\omega
is a n.s.f. weight on a von Neumann algebra, we write
\mathfrak{N}_{\omega}
for the left ideal of square integrable elements
\mathfrak{N}_{\omega}=\{x\in\M\mid\omega(x^{*}x)<+\infty\}
,
\mathsf{H}_{\omega}
for the GNS Hilbert space,
\Lambda_{\omega}
for the GNS map and
J_{\omega}
for the modular conjugation. We will typically denote the Hilbert space on which von Neumann algebra
\M
is represented (in a not necessarily standard way) by
\mathsf{H}_{\M}
, and similarly, representation
U
will typically act on
\mathsf{H}_{U}
.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
General preliminaries

We work in the setting of locally compact quantum groups, as defined by Kustermans and Vaes (
[
26
,
27
]
). A
locally compact quantum group

\mathbb{G}
is described by a von Neumann algebra
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
together with a
comultiplication

\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}
, which is an injective, normal, unital
*
-homomorphism
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
satisfying the coassociativity condition
(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}
. Furthermore, by definition on
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
there are two n.s.f. weights
\varphi_{\mathbb{G}},\psi_{\mathbb{G}}
called
left/right Haar integral
, which satisfy respectively a left/right invariance condition. An important result states that they are unique up to a positive multiple. We denote by
J_{\mathbb{G}}
the modular conjugation of
\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}
and by
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})
the GNS Hilbert space of
\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}
. One can canonically identify the GNS Hilbert space of
\psi_{\mathbb{G}}
with
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})
and then
J_{\psi_{\mathbb{G}}}
is equal to
J_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}=J_{\mathbb{G}}
up to a scalar multiple. An important role in the theory is played by two unitary operators
\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}},\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}
acting on
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})
(
Kac-Takesaki operators
). They are characterised by

((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)=\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x)),\quad((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}})\Lambda_{\psi_{\mathbb{G}}}(y)=\Lambda_{\psi_{\mathbb{G}}}((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(y))

for
\omega\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}},y\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi_{\mathbb{G}}}
. Both operators implement the comultiplication via

(2.1)

\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x)=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}=\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}*}\quad(x\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})).

With every locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
one can associate its
dual

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
, which is also a locally compact quantum group. By construction
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\mid\omega\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}\}\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, comultiplication
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is implemented by
\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[21]}
as in (
2.1
) and we can identify
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})
.
Pontryagin duality
states that the dual of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is equal to
\mathbb{G}
. One can show that
J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, hence one can consider map
R_{\mathbb{G}}=J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\cdot)^{*}J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
on
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, called the
unitary antipode
. We denote by
\nu_{\mathbb{G}}>0
the
scaling constant
. We will also use the self-adjoint unitary
u_{\mathbb{G}}=\nu_{\mathbb{G}}^{i/8}J_{\mathbb{G}}J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
and the map
j_{\mathbb{G}}=J_{\mathbb{G}}(\cdot)^{*}J_{\mathbb{G}}
, the canonical anti-isomorphism
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
. For technical reasons, it is convenient to associate with
\mathbb{G}
two more quantum groups (
[
27
, Section 4]
). The
opposite
quantum group
\mathbb{G}^{op}
with
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}^{op})=\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
and
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{op}}=\chi\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}
and the
commutant
quantum group
\mathbb{G}^{\prime}
defined by
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
and
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}=(J_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes J_{\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(J_{\mathbb{G}}(\cdot)J_{\mathbb{G}})(J_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes J_{\mathbb{G}})
. One has
(\mathbb{G}^{op})^{\prime}=(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})^{op}
, and
u_{\mathbb{G}}
implements an isomorphism between
(\mathbb{G}^{op})^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{G}
.

There is also a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebraic description of
\mathbb{G}
. One can prove that
\overline{\lin}\,\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\mid\omega\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}\}
is a weak
∗
-dense
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-subalgebra of
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
and comultiplication restricts to a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}))
(which we denote by the same symbol). We have

(2.2)

\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),\quad\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}))\subseteq\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})

and

(2.3)

\begin{split}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}&=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[23]},\quad(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]},\\
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}&=\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]},\quad(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}=\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[23]}.\end{split}

Furthermore, the unitaries
\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}},\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}
are related via

(2.4)

\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}=(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes R_{\mathbb{G}})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[21]}

(see part
(6)
of Remark
2.2
). We will also use a universal version of the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebraic picture (
[
25
]
). There is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})
together with surjective
*
-homomorphism
\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})
called the
reducing map
. A lot of the above objects have their universal versions, e.g. there is a universal version of the comultiplication
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}))
, of the unitary antipode
R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
and of the map
j_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
. They are linked to their reduced versions by
(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\pi_{\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}=\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\pi_{\mathbb{G}}
,
\pi_{\mathbb{G}}R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}=R_{\mathbb{G}}\pi_{\mathbb{G}}
and
\pi_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}j_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}=j_{\mathbb{G}}\pi_{\mathbb{G}}
. What will be important for us, is the existence of lifted versions of the canonical unitaries:

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})),\quad\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})),\quad{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))

which satisfy expected identities, e.g.
(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
,
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}=\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}
and
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}
. There are also lifted versions of
\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}
. They satisfy relations analogous to (
2.3
) and (
2.4
). On the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})
there is a canonical character
\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
called the
counit
. It is characterised by
(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}=\mathbbm{1}
. Lifted versions of the Kac-Takesaki operators allow us to introduce half-lifted comultiplications:

(2.5)

\begin{split}\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\ni x&\mapsto\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})),\\
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\ni x&\mapsto\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})).\end{split}

A (left, unitary)
representation
of
\mathbb{G}
on a Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
is a unitary element
U\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}))
satisfying
(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})U=U_{[13]}U_{[23]}
. An important result of Kustermans (
[
25
, Proposition 5.2]
) states that every representation of
\mathbb{G}
is of the form
U=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{U})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
for a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\phi_{U}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H})=\M(\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}))
. This establishes a
1
-
1
correspondence between representations of
\mathbb{G}
and (non-degenerate) representations of
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
; one can think of
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
as a full group
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra of
\mathbb{G}
. If
U,V
are representations of
\mathbb{G}
on
\mathsf{H}_{U},\mathsf{H}_{V}
, then an
intertwiner
between
U
and
V
is a bounded, linear map
T\colon\mathsf{H}_{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{V}
satisfying
(\mathbbm{1}\otimes T)U=V(\mathbbm{1}\otimes T)
. We let
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
be the category with representations of
\mathbb{G}
as objects and intertwiners as morphisms; there are natural notions of direct sum
U\oplus V
(acting on
\mathsf{H}_{U}\oplus\mathsf{H}_{V}
) and tensor product
U\otop V=U_{[12]}V_{[13]}
(acting on
\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}
).

Let
\M
be a von Neumann algebra. A (left)
action
of
\mathbb{G}
on
\M
is an injective, normal
*
-homomorphism
\alpha^{\M}\colon\M\rightarrow\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\M
satisfying
(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M})\alpha^{\M}
. We will write in this situation
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
. If
\M
acts on a Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}_{\M}
and
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
is a representation of
\mathbb{G}
on
\mathsf{H}_{\M}
which satisfies
\alpha^{\M}(m)=U^{\M*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)U^{\M}\,(m\in\M)
, then we say that
U^{\M}
(or equivalently
\phi_{\M}
)
implements

\alpha^{\M}
. There always exists at least one implementation of
\alpha^{\M}
on the standard Hilbert space
\LL^{2}(\M)
of
\M
, called the
standard
implementation (
[
47
, Definition 3.6]
, see also Lemma
7.5
and the discussion before it).

2.2.
Quantum subgroups and bicharacters

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be locally compact quantum groups.
\mathbb{H}
is said to be a (closed)
quantum subgroup
of
\mathbb{G}
(in the sense of Vaes), if there exists an injective, normal, unital
*
-homomorphism
\gamma_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
such that
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\gamma_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}=(\gamma_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}
(see
[
8
, Definition 3.1 and Theorem 3.3]
). In this situation one can find a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\theta_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H}))
satisfying
\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}^{u}\theta_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}=(\theta_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\theta_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
(i.e.
\theta_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}
is a
strong quantum homomorphism
), such that its
dual
strong quantum homomorphism
\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
, characterised by
(\theta_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}
, satisfies
\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}=\gamma_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}
. In this situation, there is an action
\Delta_{\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
implemented by
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}}
.

Assume now that
\mathbb{H}
is a quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
, and assume
\mathbb{G}
acts on a von Neumann algebra
\M
via
\alpha^{\M}
. Then we can restrict
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
to an action
\alpha^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{H}}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M
(see
[
10
, Section 6.5]
, but notice a difference in terminology). The restricted action is characterised by
(\Delta_{\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M})(\alpha^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{H}})
. One can check that if
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
and
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
is a representation of
\mathbb{G}
on
\mathsf{H}_{\M}
which implements
\alpha^{\M}
, then
U^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{H}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}){{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!}^{\mathbb{H}}}\in\Rep(\mathbb{H})
implements
\alpha^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{H}}
.

Next we introduce definitions of bicharacter and quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group (c.f.
[
32
, Definition 2.1]
).

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be locally compact quantum groups.

(1)

A unitary
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is a
bicharacter
if

(2.6)

(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[23]},\quad(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}.

(2)

A pair
(\mathbb{G},\widehat{\operatorname{R}})
is said to be a
quasi-triangular
locally compact quantum group if
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is a bicharacter satisfying

(2.7)

\widehat{\operatorname{R}}\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{*}=\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}(x)\quad(x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})).

In this case, the bicharacter
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is called an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix
.

Remark 2.2
.

(1)

Our definition of an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix differs from the one in
[
32
]
because we use “left” conventions.

(2)

Every bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
belongs to the algebra
\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
(see e.g. the discussion after
[
7
, Proposition 4.1]
) and admits a universal lift, i.e. there exists a unique unitary
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
satisfying
(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
and (a universal version of) equations (
2.6
). It will be convenient to use also the half-lifted versions
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}
. If
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix, then the lift
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}
also satisfies a universal version of (
2.7
) (
[
30
, Proposition 4.7]
,
[
32
, Proposition 2.4]
), namely

(2.8)

\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u}(x)\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}=\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}^{u}(x)\quad(x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})).

(3)

The bicharacter property (
2.6
) implies that the universal lift
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}
satisfies
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}^{u}\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=\mathbbm{1},(\textnormal{id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=\mathbbm{1}
, see
[
32
, Lemma 2.15]
.

(4)

The bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
can be seen as describing a
morphism of quantum groups

\widehat{\mathbb{H}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}
, c.f.
[
8
, Section 1.3]
.

(5)

Every bicharacter satisfies
(R_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
and
(\tau^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}_{t}\otimes\tau^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{t})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
, and similarly at the universal level (
[
30
, Proposition 3.10]
).

(6)

Above, we use the fact that
R_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
extends to a linear,
*
-preserving, antimultiplicative and strictly continuous bijection on
\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
– this also applies to other maps which are linear, antimultiplicative, bijective and
*
-preserving. A similar property holds at the von Neumann algebraic level.

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
(\mathbb{G},\widehat{\operatorname{R}})
be a quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group. Then
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
and
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}
satisfy the following version of the Yang-Baxter equation:

\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[12]}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[23]},\quad\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[23]}^{u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[13]}^{u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[12]}^{u}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[12]}^{u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[13]}^{u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{[23]}^{u}.

This result can be proven by a straightforward calculation using (
2.7
), see
[
32
, Equation (2.17)]
.

Remark 2.4
.

If
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is a bicharacter, then so is
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[21]}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})
. Its universal lift is
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u*}_{[21]}
. Furthermore, if
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix, then
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{*}_{[21]}
is also an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix. This easy observation can be used sometimes to obtain quick proofs.

In concrete calculations, it is very convenient to link the bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
with the Kac-Takesaki operator. More precisely, we have the following result (see
[
25
, Proposition 6.5]
,
[
30
, Proposition 4.2]
).

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be locally compact quantum groups and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
a bicharacter with lift
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}
. There is a unique non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\Phi\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}^{u}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}))
such that

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=(\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}^{u}\Phi=(\Phi\otimes\Phi)\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}.

2.3.
Drinfeld Double

One of the most important classes of examples of quasi-triangular quantum groups are the Drinfeld doubles.

Definition 2.6
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group. The
Drinfeld double
of
\mathbb{G}
(
[
3
, Section 8]
) is the locally compact quantum group defined via

\LL^{\infty}(D(\mathbb{G}))=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),\quad\Delta_{D(\mathbb{G})}=\operatorname{Ad}(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[32]})\circ\Delta_{\mathbb{G}\times\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}.

One can show that
D(\mathbb{G})
is unimodular and

\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbb{G})}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}Z_{[34]}^{*}\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[24]}Z_{[34]}\quad\textnormal{where}\quad Z=\nu_{\mathbb{G}}^{-\frac{i}{4}}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(J_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(J_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})

(see
[
3
, Definition 3.4, Proposition 8.1]
). Both
\mathbb{G}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
are quantum subgroups of
D(\mathbb{G})
(see e.g.
[
9
, Lemma 7.13]
), the associated maps
\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})},\gamma_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}
are given by

\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}(\widehat{x})=\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1},\quad\gamma_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}(x)=Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)Z

for
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. In other words, we have

(2.9)

\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbb{G})}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[134]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})_{[234]}.

An analogous formula holds at the half-universal level

(2.10)

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[134]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})_{[234]}

(for the sake of consistency, we consider
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})
as having two legs, and to ease the notation we write simply
\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})},\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}
). Equality (
2.10
) can be proved by applying the half-lifted comultiplication to (
2.9
).

The Drinfeld double is in a canonical way a quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group.

Proposition 2.7
.

By means of the unitary

\widehat{\operatorname{R}}=(\gamma_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}),

the pair
(D(\mathbb{G}),\widehat{\operatorname{R}})
is a quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group.

(See
[
37
, Lemma 6.11]
for a proof in a slightly different setting; note however a difference in terminology.) Another important feature of the Drinfeld double is a bijective correspondence between its actions and compatible pairs of actions of
\mathbb{G}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(see
[
34
, Definition 3.1]
and
[
34
, Proposition 3.2]
in the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebraic framework).

Definition 2.8
.

A von Neumann algebra
\M
is said to be a
\mathbb{G}
-Yetter-Drinfeld (
\mathbb{G}
-YD) von Neumann algebra if it is equipped with actions
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
,
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
satisfying the compatibility condition

(\chi\operatorname{Ad}(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}.

Proposition 2.9
.

There is a bijective correspondence between
D(\mathbb{G})
-von Neumann algebras and
\mathbb{G}
-YD von Neumann algebras. More precisely, if
\alpha^{\M}
is an action of
D(\mathbb{G})
on
\M
, then the restricted actions
\alpha^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}},\alpha^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
make
\M
into a
\mathbb{G}
-YD von Neumann algebra. Conversely, if
\M
is a
\mathbb{G}
-YD von Neumann algebra with respect to actions
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
,
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
, then
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}
is an action of
D(\mathbb{G})
. These constructions are inverse to each other.

We will need a result concerning implementations of a YD-action, which give rise to implementation of the corresponding action of
D(\mathbb{G})
.

Proposition 2.10
.

Let
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
be a
\mathbb{G}
-YD von Neumann algebra with respect to actions
\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
. Let
U^{\M,\mathbb{G}},U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
be implementations of these actions on
\mathsf{H}_{\M}
with corresponding morphisms
\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}},\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
. Assume that

(2.11)

\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[12]}=U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}.

Then there is an implementation
U^{\M}
of
D(\mathbb{G})\curvearrowright\M
with the corresponding morphism
\phi_{\M}
such that

(2.12)

U^{\M}=U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]},\quad\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}}=\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})},\quad\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}.

Conversely, if
\phi_{\M}
implements
D(\mathbb{G})\curvearrowright\M
, then
\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})},

\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}
implement the restricted actions
\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
. These implementations and the associated representations satisfy (
2.11
) and (
2.12
).

Note that (
2.11
) resembles the YD condition.

Proof.

Assume first that
\M
is a
\mathbb{G}
-YD von Neumann algebra with respect to actions implemented by
U^{\M,\mathbb{G}},U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
which satisfy (
2.11
). Consider the unitary
U^{\M}=U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}\in\LL^{\infty}(D(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
. It is a representation of
D(\mathbb{G})
:

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\Delta_{D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})U^{\M}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[32]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[25]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[32]}=\bigl(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[23]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[25]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}\bigr)_{[13245]}\\
&=\bigl(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[23]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[23]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}\bigr)_{[13245]}=\bigl(U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[25]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}\bigr)_{[13245]}\\
&=U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[25]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}=U^{\M}_{[125]}U^{\M}_{[345]},\end{split}

and it is immediate that
U^{\M}
implements the action
\alpha^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}\colon D(\mathbb{G})\curvearrowright\M
. There is a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\phi_{\M}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
such that
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})})
. Using Equation (
2.10
) we have

(2.13)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})_{[23]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})})=U^{\M}\\
&=U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})_{[23]}.\end{split}

Take
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}),\widehat{\omega}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
and apply
\omega\otimes\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id}
to (
2.13
):

(2.14)

\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})\,\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}((\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})=\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})\,\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}((\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}).

By choosing an appropriate net of functionals
\widehat{\omega}_{i}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, we can assume that
(\widehat{\omega}_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
strictly in
\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}))
(
[
25
, Proposition 4.2]
), hence (
2.14
) implies

\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})=\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})

for an arbitrary
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. By density we can conclude that
\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}=\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}}
. Equation
\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}=\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
similarly follows from (
2.14
). This proves the first part of the proposition.

Next we prove the converse: assume that action
D(\mathbb{G})\curvearrowright\M
is implemented by
U^{\M}
and
\phi_{\M}
. Define
\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}}=\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}
,
\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}
and associated representations
U^{\M,\mathbb{G}},U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
. We need to show that these implement the restricted actions
\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
, and that they satisfy (
2.12
) and (
2.11
).

First, applying
\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}
to (
2.10
) we see that
U^{\M}=U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}
. By the definition of comultiplication on
D(\mathbb{G})
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[25]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}=U^{\M}_{[125]}U^{\M}_{[345]}=(\Delta_{D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})U^{\M}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[32]}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}\times\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(U^{\M})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[32]}\\
&=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[32]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[25]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[32]}=U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[32]}U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[35]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[32]}U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}.\end{split}

Cancelling
U^{\M,\mathbb{G}}_{[15]},U^{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[45]}
and applying the flip on legs
2,3
gives us (
2.11
). Finally, we need to check that
\phi_{\M,\mathbb{G}},\phi_{\M,\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
indeed implement the restricted actions
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}
,
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
. For
m\in\M
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M})\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}(m)=(\Delta_{\mathbb{G},D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m)\\
&=(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{D(\mathbb{G})*})_{[123]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})*})_{[234]}m_{[4]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})})_{[234]}(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{D(\mathbb{G})})_{[123]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})*})_{[234]}(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{D(\mathbb{G})*})_{[14]}(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{D(\mathbb{G})*})_{[123]}m_{[4]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{D(\mathbb{G})})_{[123]}(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{D(\mathbb{G})})_{[14]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{D(\mathbb{G})})_{[234]}\\
&=U^{\M*}_{[234]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[14]}m_{[4]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[14]}U^{\M}_{[234]},\end{split}

which implies that action
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{G}}
is implemented by
\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}
. This proves the claim for
\mathbb{G}
. The reasoning for
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is analogous.

∎

3.
Braided flip operators

In this section we introduce braided flip operators, and derive their basic properties.

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be locally compact quantum groups with bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
. Let
U
be a representation of
\mathbb{G}
on
\mathsf{H}_{U}
, with the corresponding morphisms
\phi_{U}
. Similarly let
V
be a representation of
\mathbb{H}
on
\mathsf{H}_{V}
with morphism
\phi_{V}
. We will be mostly interested in the situation where representations implement actions of
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
, but the following construction works more generally.

Definition 3.1
.

We define the
braided flip operator
as the following unitary

{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})\Sigma\colon\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{V}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{U}.

Remark 3.2
.

One can also introduce another version of braided flip operators, namely
{}^{U}{\rdiagovfdiag}^{V}=({}^{V}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{U})^{*}
. These unitaries correspond to bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}
, so whether we work with
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
or
{}^{U}{\rdiagovfdiag}^{V}
is a matter of choice. In this paper we will use only
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
.

In the next section we will use operators
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
to introduce the braided tensor product of von Neumann algebras – this construction works well for a general bicharacter. If
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{H}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix, then braided flip operators have additional properties. In the next two results we will introduce the braided structure on the monoidal category
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
of unitary representations of
\mathbb{G}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Assume that
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{H}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix. Then the unitary operator
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
is a morphism, i.e.

{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]}(U\otop V)=(V\otop U)\;{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]}.

Proof.

For
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G})
we have using equation (
2.8
)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]}(U\otop V))=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(U_{[12]}V_{[13]})\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{U})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{V})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]})\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma(\phi_{U}\otimes\phi_{V})(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]})\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]})\Sigma\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}))\Sigma\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}^{u})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[23]})\Sigma\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]})(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma\\
&=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(V_{[12]}U_{[13]}){}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((V\otop U){}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]}\bigr),\end{split}

which proves the claim.
∎

Proposition 3.4
.

Assume that
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{H}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix. Then the family of morphisms
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}\,(U,V\in\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
forms a braiding, i.e.

{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V\otop W}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W})({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}\otimes\textnormal{id})\colon\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{W}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{V}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{W}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{U}

and

{}^{U\otop V}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W}=({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes{}^{V}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W})\colon\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{W}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{W}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}

for
U,V,W\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. Together with the usual (trivial) associators and unit
\mathbbm{1}\otimes 1
, this turns
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
into a braided monoidal category.

Note that the braiding on
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
depends on the
\operatorname{R}
-matrix, thus more precisely we should write that
\Rep(\mathbb{G},\widehat{\operatorname{R}})
is a braided monoidal category.

Proof.

Let us check the first relation:

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W})({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}\otimes\textnormal{id})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma)((\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma\otimes\textnormal{id})\\
&=(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[23]}(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[13]}\Sigma_{[23]}\Sigma_{[12]}=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[13]})\Sigma_{[23]}\Sigma_{[12]}\\
&=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}^{u}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma_{[23]}\Sigma_{[12]}=(\phi_{V\otop W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma_{[23]}\Sigma_{[12]}={}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V\otop W},\end{split}

where we have used
\phi_{V\otop W}=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{W})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}^{u}
. We check the second equality in a similar manner:

\begin{split}&\quad\;({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes{}^{V}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W})=((\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{V})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma)\\
&=(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[12]}(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{V})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[13]}\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{[23]}=(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U}\otimes\phi_{V})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[13]})\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{[23]}\\
&=(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U}\otimes\phi_{V})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}^{u})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{[23]}=(\phi_{W}\otimes\phi_{U\otop V})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{[23]}={}^{U\otop V}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{W}.\end{split}

We already know by Lemma
2.5
that
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
are morphisms (i.e. intertwiners):

{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]}(U\otop V)=(V\otop U)\;{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]}.

This implies

({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]})^{*}(V\otop U)=(U\otop V)({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}_{[23]})^{*}

that is,
({}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V})^{*}
is an intertwiner
\mathsf{H}_{V}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}
. Consequently
{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}
are isomorphisms in
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. It is left to check that the family
\{{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}\}_{U,V}
is natural: take equivariant maps
f\colon\mathsf{H}_{U}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{U^{\prime}},g\colon\mathsf{H}_{V}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{V^{\prime}}
. Then

(g\otimes f){}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}=(g\otimes f)(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma=(\phi_{V^{\prime}}\otimes\phi_{U^{\prime}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})\Sigma(f\otimes g)={}^{V^{\prime}}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{U^{\prime}}(f\otimes g),

which ends the proof.
∎

4.
Braided tensor product
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N

4.1.
General setup

Let
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}),\N\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N})
be von Neumann algebras,
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
locally compact quantum groups and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
a bicharacter. Assume that we have (left) actions
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\alpha^{\N}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
implemented by
U^{\M}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}_{\M}))
and
U^{\N}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}_{\N}))
. Let
\phi_{\M},\phi_{\N}
be the associated
*
-homomorphisms, i.e.
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}})
,
U^{\N}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})
; see Section
2
.

Out of this data we will construct a new von Neumann algebra
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
together with canonical embeddings
\iota_{\M},\iota_{\N}
of
\M,\N
(Theorem
4.9
). This construction does not depend on the way we represent
\M,\N
or implement the actions (Proposition
4.4
). If
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix, then
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
carries a canonical action such that
\iota_{\M},\iota_{\N}
are equivariant – in fact, a more general result is true, see Proposition
6.3
.

Recall that in the previous section we have introduced unitary maps
{}^{U^{\N}}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{U^{\M}}\colon\mathsf{H}_{\N}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}
. It will be convenient to change notation; understanding that we have fixed implementations of actions, we will write
{}^{\N}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\M}={}^{U^{\N}}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{U^{\M}}
.

Definition 4.1
.

Define normal, injective, unital
*
-homomorphisms

\begin{split}\iota_{\M}\colon\M\ni m\mapsto\iota_{\M}(m)&={}^{\N}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\M}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)({}^{\N}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\M})^{*}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}),\\
\iota_{\N}\colon\N\ni n\mapsto\iota_{\N}(n)&=\mathbbm{1}\otimes n\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}),\end{split}

and
\sigma\textsc{-wot}
-closed subspace

(4.1)

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}).

The space
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is called the
braided tensor product
of
\M
and
\N
.

Remark 4.2
.

(1)

The space
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
depends not only on algebras
\M,\N
, but also on the actions and the bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
. Most of the time this data will be clear from the context, otherwise we will indicate it in the notation.

(2)

We obtain the same space
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
if in (
4.1
) we take closure in different topologies;
wot
or
\sigma\textsc{-sot}^{*}
– this follows from
[
44
, Theorem 2.6 (iv)]
and the double commutant theorem together with Theorem
4.9
.

The main result of this section is Theorem
4.9
, which states that
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is a von Neumann algebra. While it is not too dificult to prove this statement for dual actions, it will take us some work to show in general. We first establish several preliminary results, some of them of independent interest. We begin with a useful embedding
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\hookrightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))\bar{\otimes}\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N
, and show that the braided tensor product does not depend on the choice of implementations.

Proposition 4.3
.

In
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
we have

U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))_{[24]}U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}

for any
m\in\M,n\in\N
.

Proof.

Take
m\in\M,n\in\N
and calculate

(4.2)

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))_{[24]}U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}\\
&=U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[24]}m_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}_{[24]}n_{[4]}U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}\\
&=U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[24]}m_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}_{[24]}U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}.\end{split}

Observe that

(4.3)

\begin{split}(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[23]}=\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[23]}\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}_{[12]}\quad&\Rightarrow\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[13]}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{r,u}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\\
\quad&\Rightarrow\quad\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[23]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{r,u}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[13]}.\end{split}

Applying to this equation
\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}^{r,u}\otimes\textnormal{id}
we obtain

\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[14]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[14]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[14]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[14]}

hence using again (
4.3
)

\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{u}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[14]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[14]}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[14]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[14]}

or
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[23]}^{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[13]}.

Using this we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[24]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}_{[23]})_{[124]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u*}_{[13]})_{[124]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})_{[14]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})^{*}_{[14]}\end{split}

Plugging this twice in (
4.2
) we obtain

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))_{[24]}U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}\\
&=U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})_{[14]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})^{*}_{[14]}m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})_{[14]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad U^{\M}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})^{*}_{[14]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\\
&=U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})_{[14]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})^{*}_{[14]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\end{split}

Next observe that

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\,(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u})_{[14]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{r,u}_{[13]})_{[134]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r}_{[12]})_{[143]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}\mathds{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\mathds{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]})_{[143]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}(\phi_{\N}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})_{[43]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\N*}_{[34]},\end{split}

hence

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))_{[24]}U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\N*}_{[34]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}U^{\N}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\end{split}

as claimed.
∎

4.2.
Independence of implementations

In this subsection we show that
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is, up to an isomorphism, independent of all “choices” or implementations, including passing to isomorphic quantum groups. Let us introduce the setting.

Let
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{H}_{1}
be locally compact quantum groups and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})
a bicharacter. Let
\alpha^{\M_{1}}\colon\mathbb{H}_{1}\curvearrowright\M_{1},\alpha^{\N_{1}}\colon\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\N_{1}
be left actions on von Neumann algebras
\M_{1}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{1}}),\N_{1}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N_{1}})
implemented by
U^{\M_{1}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M_{1}})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}_{1}},U^{\N_{1}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N_{1}})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
. Next, let
\mathbb{G}_{2},\mathbb{H}_{2}
be locally compact quantum groups which are isomorphic with
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{H}_{1}
. More precisely, assume that there are
*
-isomorphisms
1
1

1

We write
\theta
instead of
\pi
to avoid confusion with the reducing map.

\theta_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
,
\theta_{\mathbb{H}}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H}_{2})
which respect comultiplications. These isomorphism have reduced versions;
*
-isomorphisms
\theta_{\mathbb{G},r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
,
\theta_{\mathbb{H},r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{H}_{2})
satisfying
\pi_{\mathbb{G}_{2}}\theta_{\mathbb{G}}=\theta_{\mathbb{G},r}\pi_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
,
\pi_{\mathbb{H}_{2}}\theta_{\mathbb{H}}=\theta_{\mathbb{H},r}\pi_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}
. We also have von Neumann algebraic versions: normal
*
-isomorphisms
\gamma_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
,
\gamma_{\mathbb{H}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{2})
which agree with
\theta_{\mathbb{G},r},\theta_{\mathbb{H},r}
on
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{H}_{1})
. See
[
8
, Theorem 1.10, Theorem 3.3]
. The isomorphism
\gamma_{\mathbb{G}}
is implemented by the standard unitary
v_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\rightarrow\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
, i.e. the unique unitary implementing
\gamma_{\mathbb{G}}
:
\gamma_{\mathbb{G}}(x)=v_{\mathbb{G}}xv_{\mathbb{G}}^{*}\,(x\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))
, respecting modular conjugation
v_{\mathbb{G}}J_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}=J_{\mathbb{G}_{2}}v_{\mathbb{G}}
and standard positive cone
v_{\mathbb{G}}\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}}=\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}_{2}}}
– see
[
15
, Theorem 2.3]
and Appendix
9.1
. Similarly
\gamma_{\mathbb{H}}
is implemented by the standard unitary
v_{\mathbb{H}}
. The corresponding dual maps will be decorated with hats, e.g.
\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G},r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})
. We have
\widehat{v}_{\mathbb{G}}=v_{\mathbb{G}}^{*},\widehat{v}_{\mathbb{H}}=v_{\mathbb{H}}^{*}
(Proposition
9.2
). Let
\M_{2}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}),\N_{2}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
be von Neumann algebras isomorphic with
\M_{1},\N_{1}
via
\theta_{\M}\colon\M_{1}\rightarrow\M_{2},\theta_{\N}\colon\N_{1}\rightarrow\N_{2}
.

Assume that there is a bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{2})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
and actions
\alpha^{\M_{2}}\colon\mathbb{H}_{2}\curvearrowright\M_{2},\alpha^{\N_{2}}\colon\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\N_{2}
, and that this structure is respected by the above isomorphisms:
(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{u}
and

\alpha^{\M_{2}}\theta_{\M}=(\gamma_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\theta_{\M})\alpha^{\M_{1}},\quad\alpha^{\N_{2}}\theta_{\N}=(\gamma_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\theta_{\N})\alpha^{\N_{1}}.

In this situation we can form two braided tensor products,
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{1}}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{1}})
and
\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
.

Proposition 4.4
.

(1)

There is a unique completely isometric isomorphism
\Upsilon\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
which is a
\sigma
-
wot
homeomorphism and satisfies
\Upsilon(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))=\iota_{\M_{2}}(\theta_{\M}(m))\iota_{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n))
for
m\in\M_{1},n\in\N_{1}
.

(2)

The space
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
is closed under multiplication if and only if it is closed under adjoints. The same is true for
\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
.

(3)

If one of the spaces
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1},\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
is closed under multiplication, then so is the other. In this case
\Upsilon
is a
*
-isomorphism.

The main usefulness of this result comes from the fact that quite often it is not difficult to find “an” implementation, but it’s more work to show that it is the standard one. This proposition is also an important ingredient in the proof of Theorem
4.9
.

Proof.

For any
m\in\M_{1},n\in\N_{1}
, we have by Proposition
4.3

(4.4)

U^{\N_{1}*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}U^{\M_{1}*}_{[12]}(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))_{[24]}U^{\M_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}^{*}U^{\N_{1}}_{[34]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}\alpha^{\M_{1}}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N_{1}}(n)_{[34]}

in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M_{1}}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{1}})
and

(4.5)

U^{\N_{2}*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}U^{\M_{2}*}_{[12]}(\iota_{\M_{2}}(\theta_{\M}(m))\iota_{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n)))_{[24]}U^{\M_{2}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}^{*}U^{\N_{2}}_{[34]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}\alpha^{\M_{2}}(\theta_{\M}(m))_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n))_{[34]}

in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{2})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
.

Consider three mappings

\Upsilon_{1}\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\ni\mathbf{X}\mapsto U^{\N_{1}*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}U^{\M_{1}*}_{[12]}\mathbf{X}_{[24]}U^{\M_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}^{*}U^{\N_{1}}_{[34]}\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M_{1}}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{1}}),

next

\operatorname{Ad}(v_{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\theta_{\M}\otimes\Ad(v_{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\theta_{\N}\colon\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\M_{1}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{2}))\bar{\otimes}\M_{2}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\bar{\otimes}\N_{2}

and finally

\Upsilon_{2}=\operatorname{Ad}\bigl(U^{\M_{2}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}^{*}U^{\N_{2}}_{[34]}\bigr)\in\Aut\bigl(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{2})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})\bigr).

Clearly all these maps are completely isometric and
\sigma\textsc{-wot}
-continuous. Equation (
4.4
) shows that image of
\Upsilon_{1}
lies in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\M_{1}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\N_{1}
– hence we can consider
\Upsilon_{1}
as a map

\Upsilon_{1}\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\M_{1}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\N_{1}.

Before we go further we need to make another observation. By our assumption we have
(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{u}
. Applying reducing morphisms gives

(4.6)

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}=(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H},r}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G},r})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}=(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}=(\widehat{v}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\widehat{v}_{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}(\widehat{v}_{\mathbb{H}}^{*}\otimes\widehat{v}_{\mathbb{G}}^{*})=(v^{*}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes v^{*}_{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2}(v_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes v_{\mathbb{G}}).

Consider the composition

\Upsilon_{3}=\Upsilon_{2}(\Ad(v_{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\theta_{\M}\otimes\Ad(v_{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\theta_{\N})\Upsilon_{1}\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}_{2})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}}).

It is completely isometric and
\sigma\textsc{-wot}
-continuous, as a composition of such maps. Furthermore using (
4.4
), (
4.6
) and (
4.5
) we have

(4.7)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Upsilon_{3}(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))=\Upsilon_{2}(\Ad(v_{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\theta_{\M}\otimes\Ad(v_{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\theta_{\N})\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}\alpha^{\M_{1}}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N_{1}}(n)_{[34]}\bigr)\\
&=\Upsilon_{2}\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}(\gamma_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\theta_{\M})\alpha^{\M_{1}}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}^{*}(\gamma_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\theta_{\N})\alpha^{\N_{1}}(n)_{[34]}\bigr)\\
&=\Upsilon_{2}\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}\alpha^{\M_{2}}(\theta_{\M}(m))_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{2[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n))_{[34]}\bigr)=(\iota_{\M_{1}}(\theta_{\M}(m))\iota_{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n)))_{[24]}.\end{split}

Thus the image of
\Upsilon_{3}
lies in
\mathbb{C}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}})\bar{\otimes}\mathbb{C}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
. Let
\Upsilon_{4}\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
be the map
\Upsilon_{3}
composed with the
*
-isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}})\bar{\otimes}\mathbb{C}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})\simeq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}})\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
. Then (
4.7
) gives

\Upsilon_{4}(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))=\iota_{\M_{2}}(\theta_{\M}(m))\iota_{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n)).

In particular (by
\sigma
-
wot
-continuity and closedness) we can consider
\Upsilon_{4}
as a map

\Upsilon_{4}\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}

which is
\sigma
-
wot
-continuous and completely isometric.

Reversing this construction, i.e. starting with
\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
, we obtain a completely isometric,
\sigma
-
wot
-continuous map
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{4}\colon\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}\rightarrow\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
which is given by
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{4}(\iota_{\M_{2}}(\theta_{\M}(m))\iota_{\N_{2}}(\theta_{\N}(n)))=\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{N_{1}}(n)
. Clearly
\Upsilon_{4}
and
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{4}
are each other inverses. Uniqueness is also clear. This proves the first claim.

Since
(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))^{*}=\iota_{\N_{1}}(n^{*})\iota_{\M_{1}}(m^{*})
, we have that if
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
is closed under multiplication, it is also closed under adjoints. Conversely, assume
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
is closed under adjoints. Since multiplication in
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
is separately
\sigma
-
wot
-continuous, we will have that
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
is closed under multiplication if we can show that it contains all products
(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))(\iota_{\M_{1}}(\widetilde{m})\iota_{\N_{1}}(\widetilde{n}))
with
m,\widetilde{m}\in\M_{1},n,\widetilde{n}\in\N_{1}
. But

(\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)\iota_{\N_{1}}(n))(\iota_{\M_{1}}(\widetilde{m})\iota_{\N_{1}}(\widetilde{n}))=\iota_{\M_{1}}(m)(\iota_{\M_{1}}(\widetilde{m}^{*})\iota_{\N_{1}}(n^{*}))^{*}\iota_{\N_{1}}(\widetilde{n}),

by assumption
(\iota_{\M_{1}}(\widetilde{m}^{*})\iota_{\N_{1}}(n^{*}))^{*}\in\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
and clearly
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
is closed under left-multiplication by
\iota_{\M_{1}}(\M_{1})
and right multiplication by
\iota_{\N_{1}}(\N_{1})
. The proof for
\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
is analogous. This proves the second point.

Finally, assume that
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}
is a von Neumann algebra. By construction,
\Upsilon_{4}\colon\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1}\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N_{2}})
is
*
-preserving (it is a composition of maps which are clearly
*
-preserving). Take
x=\Upsilon_{4}(y)\in\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
. Then we have

x^{*}=\Upsilon_{4}(y)^{*}=\Upsilon_{4}(y^{*})\in\Upsilon_{4}(\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{1})=\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}.

This shows that
\M_{2}\overline{\boxtimes}\N_{2}
is closed under adjoint. The second point shows that it is also closed under multiplication, hence it is a von Neumann algebra. An analogous argument (using
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{4}
) proves the converse. In this situation,
\Upsilon_{4}
is a
*
-homomorphism by construction.
∎

4.3.
Universal lift of an action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M

In the proof of Theorem
4.9
we will need an auxilliary construction, which can be thought of as a lift of an action to the universal level.

In what follows, we will use the notion of enveloping von Neumann algebra. Recall that if
\mathcal{A}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra, then the second dual
\mathcal{A}^{**}
has the structure of a von Neumann algebra,
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{**}
is a weak
∗
-dense
*
-subalgebra and
\mathcal{A}^{**}
has the following universal property: for every von Neumann algebra
\operatorname{P}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\operatorname{P}})
and a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\pi\colon\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{\operatorname{P}})
with
\pi(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\operatorname{P}
, there exists a unique extension of
\pi
to a normal, unital
*
-homomorphism
\pi^{\vN}\colon\mathcal{A}^{**}\rightarrow\operatorname{P}
(
[
35
, Theorem 3.7.7, Proposition 3.7.8]
). Note also that
\M(\mathcal{A})
can be identified with the two sided multipliers of the image of
\mathcal{A}
in any faithful, non-degenerate representation (
[
35
, Proposition 3.12.3]
), in particular
\M(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{**}
.

Remark 4.5
.

Let us note that if
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\M(\mathcal{B})
is a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism, then the normal extension
\mathcal{A}^{**}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}^{**}
and the strict extension
\M(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\M(\mathcal{B})\subseteq\mathcal{B}^{**}
agree on
\M(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{**}
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group. We will use a von-Neumann-algebraic version of half-lifted comultiplications. Recall that we have a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\ni x\mapsto\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})).

Since
\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
can be seen as an element of
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, we have a natural extension of
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r}
to a normal, unital
*
-homomorphism
