\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r,\vN}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\ni x\mapsto\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})

(it follows from
w^{*}
-density of
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})
in
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
that the co-domain is correct). Similarly,
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}
admits an extension to a normal, unital
*
-homomorphism

\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u,\vN}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\ni x\mapsto\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}.

Next assume that
\mathbb{G}
acts on a von Neumann algebra
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
and
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}_{\M}))
is a representation implementing the action
\alpha^{\M}
. As mentioned above, we can extend uniquely the (non-degenerate)
*
-homomorphism
\phi_{\M}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
to a normal, unital
*
-homomorphism
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
. We denote this extension by
\phi_{\M}^{\vN}
. We will use the same notation also for other normal maps given by the universal property of the enveloping von Neumann algebra.

Define the normal, unital, injective
*
-homomorphism
2
2

2

Note that
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})
is an element of
\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}_{\M}))\subseteq\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
, so the multiplication (
4.8
) makes sense in
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
. We can also see
{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}
as an element of
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
but we have
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}^{\vN})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})
, by Remark
4.5
.

(4.8)

\alpha^{\M,u}\colon\M\ni m\mapsto(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\M.

We can think of
\alpha^{\M,u}
as a universal lift of the action
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
. Note that a priori, the above map lands in
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
, but in the next two lemmas we show that
\alpha^{\M,u}
indeed has the above codomain, does not depend on the choice of implementation and satisfies a version of the action condition.

Lemma 4.6
.

For
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}),m\in\M
we have

(4.9)

(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m))(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})=\bigl((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta^{r,u,\vN}_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id}\bigr)\alpha^{\M}(m)

in
\mathrm{C}^{u}_{0}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
. Furthermore

\alpha^{\M,u}(m)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\M.

Proof.

The desired equation follows from a straightforward calculation:

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m))(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}\bigr)\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[23]}m_{[3]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}\bigr)\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[213]}m_{[3]}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[213]}\bigr)\\
&=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})m_{[3]}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})\bigr)\\
&=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u,\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})\bigr)\\
&=((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u,\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m).\end{split}

Recall (
[
22
, Proposition 2.9]
) that in the von Neumann algebraic context the Podleś condition

\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\alpha^{\M}(\M)(\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathbbm{1})=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\M

is always satisfied. Consequently,
\M=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\rho\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m)\,|\,\rho\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}),m\in\M\}
. The last claim easily follows.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

The map
\alpha^{\M,u}
satisfies

(1)

(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M})\alpha^{\M,u}=(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r,\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}
,

(2)

(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M,u})\alpha^{\M}=(\Delta^{r,u,\vN}_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}
.

Moreover,
\alpha^{\M,u}
does not depend on the choice of implementation
U^{\M}
.

Proof.

Take
m\in\M
and calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M})\alpha^{\M,u}(m)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[13]}m_{[3]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[23]}\\
&=(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})m_{[3]}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})=(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,r,\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m)\end{split}

which proves the first claim. Since
\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M}
is injective, the last claim follows. Finally, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M,u})\alpha^{\M}(m)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[13]}m_{[3]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[23]}\\
&=(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})m_{[3]}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{r,u}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})=(\Delta^{r,u,\vN}_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m).\end{split}

∎

We will also need a version of the Podleś condition for the universal lift
\alpha^{\M,u}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

For any action
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
we have
\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}(\mathrm{C}^{u}_{0}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\M,u}(\M)=\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\M
.

The proof of this proposition is a technical modification of the proof of
[
22
, Proposition 2.9]
.

Proof.

First we show the equality (c.f.
[
22
, Equality (2.9)]
and
[
1
, Proposition 3.6]
)

(4.10)

\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes b)\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}=\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})).

We calculate the left hand side as follows, using in the 3rd equality that
\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is a unitary in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
:

(4.11)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes b)\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}b_{[2]})\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}_{[23]}\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}(b\otimes a)_{[23]})\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},a,b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}_{[23]}\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]}(b\otimes a)_{[23]})\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},a,b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(a_{[3]}\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}_{[23]}b_{[2]}\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]})\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},a,b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})(a_{[2]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[23]}b_{[3]}\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]})\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},a,b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}.\end{split}

Now, recall that regularity at the von Neumann algebraic level is always satisfied:

\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\,\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(\Sigma\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})\mid\omega\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}\}=\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),

where
\Sigma
is the flip map (see the last equation in the proof of
[
2
, Proposition 2.9]
and
[
49
, Proposition 2.5]
). Consequently

\begin{split}&\quad\;\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\,\{(a\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes b)\mid a,b\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\,\{(|\xi\rangle\langle\eta|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes|\zeta\rangle\langle\varkappa|)\mid\xi,\eta,\zeta,\varkappa\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\,\{\Sigma(\mathbbm{1}\otimes|\xi\rangle\langle\eta|)(\Sigma\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes|\zeta\rangle\langle\varkappa|)\mid\xi,\eta,\zeta,\varkappa\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\,\{\Sigma((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(\Sigma\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})\otimes a)\mid\omega\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},a\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\Sigma(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})))=\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})).\end{split}

With this we continue (
4.11
):

\begin{split}&\quad\;\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes b)\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})((a\otimes b)_{[23]}\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]})\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},a,b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}})\otimes b\mid\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}=\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\end{split}

which proves (
4.10
). Consider the map
R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
acting on
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})
and its double dual, denoted
R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,\vN}
. It is the unique normal extension of
R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
to
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}
and it is bijective, linear,
*
-preserving and antimultiplicative. Applying (see Remark
2.2
)
R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u,\vN}\otimes J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\cdot)^{*}J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
and the flip to (
4.10
) we obtain

\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(b\otimes\mathbbm{1})(R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[21]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}=\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}.

Since
(R_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[21]}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
, applying the adjoint gives

(4.12)

\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}(b\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\}=\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}.

Using the weak Podleś condition for
\alpha^{\M}
(
[
22
, Corollary 2.7]
), Lemma
4.7
and equation (
4.12
), we can prove the Podleś condition for
\alpha^{\M,u}
:

\begin{split}&\quad\;\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\M,u}(\M)\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\M,u}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m))\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(x_{[2]}(\Delta^{r,u,\vN}_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m))\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})((b\otimes x)_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[12]})\mid b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})),x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[13]}x_{[2]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[12]}b_{[1]})\mid b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})),x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[13]}(b\otimes x)_{[12]}\mid b\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})),x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,w^{*}}\{x\otimes(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}(m)\mid x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**},\omega\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*},m\in\M\}=\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\M.\end{split}

∎

4.4.
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is a von Neumann algebra

As before, let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be locally compact quantum groups with bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
and actions on von Neumann algebras
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\alpha^{\N}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
. Represent
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}),\N\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N})
and let the actions be implemented by
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}},U^{\N}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
. Lemma
2.5
gives us a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\Phi\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}))
which commutes with comultiplications, such that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=(\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}
. The main result of this section is the following theorem.

Theorem 4.9
.

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
is a von Neumann algebra.

We note that Proposition
4.4
shows that
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
does not depend on the choices of implementations (only on the actions
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
and the bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
). Before we prove Theorem
4.9
, we need to establish several auxilliary results. First, using the bicharacter
\mathcal{X}
we introduce an action
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
.

Consider the dual morphism to
\Phi
, which is a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\widehat{\Phi}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
, uniquely characterised by
(\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\Phi}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}
(see
[
30
, Corollary 4.3]
). By the universal property of the double dual (see Section
4.3
), we can extend
\widehat{\Phi}
to a unital normal
*
-homomorphism
\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**}\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
. Similarly, we extend the reducing map to a unital normal
*
-homomorphism
\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{\vN}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Next define

\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M,u}\colon\M\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\M.

By construction,
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is a unital normal
*
-homomorphism.

Proposition 4.10
.

We have that
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
defines an action
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\M
and

\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})

for
m\in\M
.

Proof.

Seeing
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})
as an element of
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
or
\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}_{\M}))
, we have by Remark
4.5

(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}))=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})

(to see this, apply an arbitrary normal functional to the right leg) and

\begin{split}\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)&=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})\bigr)\\
&=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})\end{split}

for
m\in\M
. This also shows that
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is injective. Next we check the action condition: for an arbitary
m\in\M
we calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)\\
&=(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})\bigr)\\
&=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}_{[13]}{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}_{[23]})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}_{[13]}{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}_{[23]})\\
&=(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}_{[23]}(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}_{[13]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})_{[13]}(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})_{[23]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m).\end{split}

∎

Next we record a general lemma, which we will use in the case of action
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
. It is well known in the community, but we record a short proof for the convenience of the reader (c.f.
[
22
, Equation (2.3)]
).

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be a locally compact quantum group acting on a von Neumann algebra
\operatorname{P}
via
\alpha^{\operatorname{P}}\colon\mathbb{K}\curvearrowright\operatorname{P}
. We have

\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{K}))\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{P}=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(T\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\operatorname{P}}(p)\mid p\in\operatorname{P},T\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{K}))\}.

Proof.

Clearly we have
\supseteq
, we need to show the converse inclusion
\subseteq
. The Podleś condition for
\alpha^{\operatorname{P}}
(
[
22
, Proposition 2.9]
) reads

(4.13)

\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{P}=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\operatorname{P}}(p)\mid x\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}),m\in\operatorname{P}\}.

Take
T\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{K})),p\in\operatorname{P}
. By
[
49
, Proposition 2.5]
we can choose elements
x_{i,k}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}),\widehat{x}_{i,k}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{K}})\,(i\in I,1\leq k\leq K_{i})
such that
\sum_{k=1}^{K_{i}}\widehat{x}_{i,k}x_{i,k}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}T
, with convergence in
\sigma
-
wot
. Next, for
i\in I,1\leq k\leq K_{i}
we can by (
4.13
) find
x_{i,k,j,l}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}),p_{i,k,j,l}\in\operatorname{P}\,(j\in J_{i,k},1\leq l\leq L_{i,k,j})
so that
\sum_{l=1}^{L_{i,k,j}}(x_{i,k,j,l}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\operatorname{P}}(p_{i,k,j,l})\xrightarrow[j\in J_{i,k}]{}x_{i,k}\otimes p
again in
\sigma
-
wot
. We obtain

\begin{split}T\otimes p&=\underset{i\in I}{\sigma\textsc{-wot}\textsc{-}\!\lim}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{i}}(\widehat{x}_{i,k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(x_{i,k}\otimes p)=\underset{i\in I}{\sigma\textsc{-wot}\textsc{-}\!\lim}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{i}}\underset{j\in J_{i,k}}{\sigma\textsc{-wot}\textsc{-}\!\lim}\sum_{l=1}^{L_{i,k,j}}(\widehat{x}_{i,k}x_{i,k,j,l}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\operatorname{P}}(p_{i,k,j,l}),\end{split}

which ends the proof.
∎

Our proof of Theorem
4.9
will rely on the biduality theorem
[
47
, Theorem 2.6]
, which we will use to reduce the general situation to the case of dual actions. Let us recall its content in the case of the action
\alpha^{\N}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
.

Consider the crossed product von Neumann algebra, defined as

\N^{\ltimes}=\mathbb{G}\ltimes\N=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\N}(n)\mid\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),n\in\N\}\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N

(see
[
47
, Definition 2.1, Lemma 3.3]
). On this von Neumann algebra we have an action
\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes}}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}\curvearrowright\N^{\ltimes}
uniquely determined by

\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes}}(\alpha^{\N}(n))=\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha^{\N}(n),\quad\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes}}(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})=\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}(\widehat{x})\otimes\mathbbm{1},\quad(n\in\N,\widehat{x}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})).

It is implemented by

(4.14)

\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\pi_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}))\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}}=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op})\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})

(
[
47
, Proposition 2.2]
, see also
[
27
, Section 4]
). The action
\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes}}
is sometimes called the
dual action
. We can consider the bidual crossed product
\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}=\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op}\ltimes(\mathbb{G}\ltimes\N)\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N
, which is equipped with the dual action
\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}}\colon(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op})^{\wedge op}\curvearrowright\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}
. Observe that on
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N
we have the obvious
\mathbb{G}
-action
\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}=(\chi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\N})
(where
\chi
is the flip map). The biduality theorem states that

\Psi\colon\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N\ni z\mapsto(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\N})(z)(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\in\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}

is a well defined isomorphism. Furthermore,
\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[21]}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
is a
\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}
-cocycle, hence we can define the action

\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}=\Ad(\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[21]}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\N}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N

which is isomorphic to the bidual action
\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}}
in the sense that

(4.15)

\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}}(\Psi(z))=(j_{\mathbb{G}}R_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\Psi)(\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}(z))\quad(z\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N).

Note that
(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{op})^{\wedge op}={\mathbb{G}^{op}}^{\prime}
and
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{\mathbb{G}})=j_{\mathbb{G}}R_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}({\mathbb{G}^{op}}^{\prime})
is the von Neumann algebraic version of isomorphism
\mathbb{G}\simeq{\mathbb{G}^{op}}^{\prime}
(see
[
27
, Section 4]
and Section
2
).

In order to work with the actions
\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N},\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}
we need their concrete implementations.

Lemma 4.12
.

(1)

The representation
U^{\N}_{[13]}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
implements
\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}
and the corresponding map is
\phi_{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}=\mathbbm{1}\otimes\phi_{\N}
.

(2)

The representation
U^{\N}_{[13]}\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[21]}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
implements
\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}
and the corresponding map is
\phi_{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,+}=(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u}
.

Proof.

Take
x\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N
. We have

\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}(x)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\alpha^{\N})(x)_{[213]}=U^{\N*}_{[13]}x_{[23]}U^{\N}_{[13]},

hence
U^{\N}_{[13]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\phi_{\N}))\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
implements
\alpha^{\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}
. Moreover,

\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}(x)=\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[21]}U^{\N*}_{[13]}x_{[23]}U^{\N}_{[13]}\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[21]}

so the unitary
U^{\N}_{[13]}\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[21]}
implements the action
\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}
. We need to check that it is equal to
(\phi_{\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),+}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{V}^{\mathbb{G}})
(which will also show that it is a representation). This follows from a straightforward calculation:

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\N}_{[13]}\mathrm{V}^{\mathbb{G}}_{[21]}=U^{\N}_{[13]}(R_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})_{[12]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})_{[12]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,+})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}.\end{split}

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.9
.

We divide the proof into several claims. First we prove the theorem under the assumption that the action on
\N
is the dual action.

Claim 1.
If
(\N,\alpha^{\N})=(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}\ltimes\widetilde{\N},\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}^{\ltimes}})
is the dual action for some left action
\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}\curvearrowright\widetilde{\N}
, then
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is a von Neumann algebra.

Proof of Claim 1
. By Proposition
4.4
we can freely use the implementation of the action
\alpha^{\N}
on
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\widetilde{\N}}
as in (
4.14
), hence

\begin{split}\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\iota_{\M}(\M)\iota_{\N}(\N)\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}((m\otimes\mathbbm{1})\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}_{[12]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}\\
&\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\M)\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\LL^{2}(\widetilde{\N})).\end{split}

Take
m\in\M,\eta,\zeta\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})
, let
x=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})
be the associated element of
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}^{op})
and let
\{\xi_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be an orthonormal basis in
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})
. Observe that

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[12]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}=(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]})_{[132]}=(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[13]})_{[132]}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[12]}.

Using this twice, we can write
\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})
in a different way:

(4.16)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}(m\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[2]}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[12]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]})_{[12]}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*}_{[12]})_{[12]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})\bigl((\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*})_{[13]}\bigr)_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}^{*}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})\bigl(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[23]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r}_{[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r*})_{[13]}\bigr)_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}^{*}\\
&=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\xi_{\lambda}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta})\\
&\hskip 91.04872pt\bigl((\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}\bigr)_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}_{[12]}\end{split}

(the above series converges in
\sigma
-
wot
). Observe that

(4.17)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathds{W}^{\mathbb{G}})^{*}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}})^{*}\\
&=\bigl((\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})\bigr)_{[21]}\\
&=\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)_{[21]},\end{split}

which belongs to
\M\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
(Proposition
4.10
). Hence

(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta})\bigl((\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}\bigr)=(\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)\in\M.

We can thus continue (
4.16
) as follows:

(4.18)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\\
&=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\xi_{\lambda}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}(\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})^{*}_{[12]}\\
&=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\iota_{\N}\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\xi_{\lambda}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\otimes\mathbbm{1}\bigr)\iota_{\M}\bigl((\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)\bigr).\end{split}

Next we consider elements of
\N=\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}\ltimes\widetilde{\N}
of the form
\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})
for
\widetilde{n}\in\widetilde{\N}
. Using
\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})\bar{\otimes}\widetilde{\N}
and
(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
we have

(4.19)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n}))=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}(m\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}^{*}\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})_{[23]}\\
&=\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})_{[23]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}(m\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}^{*}=\iota_{\N}(\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n}))\iota_{\M}(m).\end{split}

Since
\N=\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}\ltimes\widetilde{\N}=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})\mid\widetilde{n}\in\widetilde{\N},x\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\}
, any element of
\N
can be approximated (in
\sigma
-
wot
) by sums of elements of the form
((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})
. Using equations (
4.18
), (
4.19
) we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\bigl(\iota_{\M}(m)\,\iota_{\N}\bigl(((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\zeta})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n})\bigr)\bigr)^{*}\\
&=\bigl(\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\iota_{\N}\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\xi_{\lambda}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\otimes\mathbbm{1}\bigr)\,\iota_{\M}\bigl((\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)\bigr)\,\iota_{\N}(\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n}))\bigr)^{*}\\
&=\bigl(\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\iota_{\N}\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\xi_{\lambda}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\otimes\mathbbm{1}\bigr)\,\iota_{\N}(\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n}))\,\iota_{\M}\bigl((\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\zeta}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)\bigr)\,\bigr)^{*}\\
&=\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\,\iota_{\M}\bigl((\omega_{\zeta,\xi_{\lambda}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m^{*})\bigr)\,\iota_{\N}\bigl(\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\widetilde{n}^{*})\,((\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{\xi_{\lambda},\eta})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\mathbbm{1})\bigr)\in\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N.\end{split}

Hence we can conclude that
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is closed under taking adjoints. In Proposition
4.4
we have checked that this is enough to conclude that
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is a von Neumann algebra. This proves Claim 1. ∎

Now we go back to the situation where
\alpha^{\N},\alpha^{\M}
are arbitrary left actions. Recall that quantum groups
\mathbb{G}
and
{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op}={\mathbb{G}^{op}}^{\prime}
are isomorphic; at the von Neumann algebraic level via
\operatorname{Ad}(u_{\mathbb{G}})=j_{\mathbb{G}}R_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}({\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op})
. Observe that
({\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op})^{\wedge}={(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime}}^{op}
. Out of bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
we construct new bicharacter

(\textnormal{id}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(({\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op})^{\wedge}).

Claim 2.
The braided tensor product
(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\N^{\ltimes\ltimes},\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}})
, constructed with the bicharacter
(\textnormal{id}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
, is a von Neumann algebra.

Claim 2 follows immediately from Claim 1, since
(\N^{\ltimes\ltimes},\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}})
is a dual action.

In the rest of the proof we will work only with the actions of
\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}
and the bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
.

Claim 3.

(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha_{+}^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})
is a von Neumann algebra.

Proof of Claim 3.
The biduality theorem tells us that the bidual action
\alpha^{\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}}\colon{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op}\curvearrowright\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}
and the action
\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}_{+}\colon

\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N
are isomorphic (using the canonical isomorphism
\mathbb{G}\simeq{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op}
), see (
4.15
). Consequently Claim 3 follows from Claim 2 and Proposition
4.4
. Indeed, we keep the same quantum group
\mathbb{H}
, but pass to the isomorphic quantum group
\mathbb{G}\simeq{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}^{op}
and von Neumann algebra
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N\simeq\N^{\ltimes\ltimes}
using isomorphisms which map between the bicharacters
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
and
(\textnormal{id}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
.∎

Claim 4.

(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})
is a von Neumann algebra.

Proof of Claim 4.
We will show

(4.20)

(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})=(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha_{+}^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})

(as an equality of subspaces in
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
) which reduces Claim 4 to Claim 3. Using Lemma
4.12
we have a concrete description of these braided tensor products as

(4.21)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\mid m\in\M,T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),n\in\N\}\end{split}

and

(4.22)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha_{+}^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\mid m\in\M,T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),n\in\N\}\end{split}

Take a generating element in (
4.22
) for
m\in\M,T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),n\in\N
and calculate

(4.23)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[12]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[12]}^{*}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}.\end{split}

Using (
4.17
) we have

(4.24)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\phi_{\M}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[12]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[12]}^{*}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})\bigl((\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u,r})_{[12]}^{*}\bigr)\\
&=(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)_{[21]}\in\M\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime}.\end{split}

The last containment allows us to write
(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m)_{[21]}=\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}\otimes\widehat{x}^{\prime}_{i}
for some
m_{i}\in\M,\widehat{x}^{\prime}_{i}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime}
(convergence in
\sigma
-
wot
) and consequently continue (
4.23
) as

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\\
&=\sum_{i\in I}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}(m_{i}\otimes\widehat{x}^{\prime}_{i})_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\\
&=\sum_{i\in I}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}m_{i[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(\widehat{x}^{\prime}_{i}T\otimes n)_{[23]}.\end{split}

This shows the inclusion
\supseteq
in (
4.20
).

Conversely, take as before
m\in\M,T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),n\in\N
and consider a generating element of (
4.21
),

(4.25)

(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}(m\otimes T)_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}n_{[3]}.

Thanks to Lemma
4.11
for the action
\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
, upon applying adjoint, flip and automorphism
\Ad(u_{\mathbb{G}})
, we can write
m\otimes T
as

m\otimes T=\sum_{j\in J}(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m_{j})_{[21]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes T_{j})

(convergence in
\sigma
-
wot
) for some
T_{j}\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),m_{j}\in\M
. Consequently, using again (
4.24
) we continue (
4.25
):

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\\
&=\sum_{j\in J}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(m_{j})_{[21]}T_{j[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}n_{[3]}\\
&=\sum_{j\in J}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[12]}m_{j[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[12]}^{*}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T_{j}\otimes n)_{[23]}\\
&=\sum_{j\in J}(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})m_{j[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}R_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(T_{j}\otimes n)_{[23]}\end{split}

which clearly belongs to (
4.22
). We conclude that the two vector spaces (
4.21
) and (
4.22
) are equal.∎

Claim 5.

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is a von Neumann algebra.

Proof of Claim 5.
We have established in Lemma
4.12
that the morphism associated with action
\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N}
is
\mathbbm{1}\otimes\phi_{\N}
. Consequently, Claim 5 tells us that the subspace

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\M,\alpha^{\M})\overline{\boxtimes}(\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,\alpha^{\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N})\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[13]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[23]}\mid m\in\M,T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),n\in\N\}\end{split}

is a von Neumann algebra in
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
. Upon applying the flip map to the first two
legs we see that

\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[23]}m_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})_{[23]}^{*}(T\otimes n)_{[13]}\mid m\in\M,T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),n\in\N\}

is a von Neumann algebra in
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
. This space is clearly equal to

\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{T\otimes x\mid T\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})),x\in\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\}

and Claim 5 follows.
∎∎

At the end of this section, let us record two easy results, the first concerned with quantum subgroups and the second with the associated bicharacter.

4.5.
Braided tensor product and quantum subgroups

Assume that
\mathbb{H}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}
are locally compact quantum groups with quantum subgroups, and that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})
are bicharacters such that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=(\gamma_{\mathbb{H}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{H}}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1})
. Assume that we have actions
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{H}}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{G}}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
and corresponding restricted actions
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}=(\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{H}})\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}
,
\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}=(\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{G}})\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
. Next, represent
\M,\N
on some Hilbert spaces and let
\phi_{\M},\phi_{\N}
be implementations of the actions of
\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}
. Recall that
\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{H}}
and
\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}
implement actions of
\mathbb{H}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{1}
(see Section
2
). In this situation, we can form two braided tensor products:
\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}\N
with respect to the actions of
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
and bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
, and
\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}}\N
with respect to
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{H}_{1},\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}
.

Proposition 4.13
.

We have
\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}\N=\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}}\N
with an equality of canonical embeddings of
\M,\N
.

Proof.

Observe first that the universal lifts
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u},\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}_{1}
of bicharacters satisfy

(4.26)

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{H}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{H}}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{u}).

Indeed, both of the above unitary operators are bicharacters and are lifts of
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
, hence the claim follows from the uniqueness part of
[
30
, Proposition 4.7]
. Now the equality
\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}\N=\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{1}}\N
is an immediate consequence of definitions.
∎

4.6.
Concerning the bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be locally compact quantum groups,
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
a bicharacter and
\M,\N
von Neumann algebras equipped with actions
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
. Then
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})
is a bicharacter (Remark
2.4
) and we can consider two braided tensor products,
\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}\N
and
\N\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}\M
. To avoid ambiguity, let us denote the corresponding embeddings by
\iota_{\M}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}},\iota_{\N}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\iota_{\N}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}},\iota_{\M}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}
.

Proposition 4.14
.

The map

\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}\N\ni\iota_{\M}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(m)\iota_{\N}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(n)\mapsto\iota_{\M}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}(m)\iota_{\N}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}(n)\in\N\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}\M.

extends to a well defined
*
-isomorphism.

Proof.

Choose arbitary representations of
\M,\N
and implementations
\phi_{\M},\phi_{\N}
of the actions. By definition, for
m\in\M,n\in\N
we have

\iota_{\M}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(m)\iota_{\N}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(n)=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)\in\M\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}\N.

Applying
\operatorname{Ad}\bigl((\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}\bigr)
and the tensor flip to this element gives

(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u*}_{[21]})(n\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}_{[21]})=\iota_{\M}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}(m)\iota_{\N}^{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[21]}^{*}}(n)\in\N\overline{\boxtimes}_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[21]}}\M,

which proves the claim.
∎

5.
Maps on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N

Let
\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}
be locally compact quantum groups acting on von Neumann algebras
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\widetilde{\M},\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N,\widetilde{\N}
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
a bicharacter. In this section we prove that we can take the “braided tensor product” of equivariant maps, and deduce from this certain permanence of approximation properties. Proposition
8.3
shows that the braided tensor product of non-equivariant maps might not exist.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\vartheta_{1}\colon\M\rightarrow\widetilde{\M}
,
\vartheta_{2}\colon\N\rightarrow\widetilde{\N}
be maps which are normal, completely bounded and equivariant. There exists a unique normal linear map

\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}\colon\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\ni\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)\mapsto\iota_{\widetilde{\M}}(\vartheta_{1}(m))\iota_{\widetilde{\N}}(\vartheta_{2}(n))\in\widetilde{\M}\overline{\boxtimes}\widetilde{\N}.

It is completely bounded with
\|\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}\|_{cb}\leq\|\vartheta_{1}\|_{cb}\|\vartheta_{2}\|_{cb}
. If
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}
are unital / completely positive /
*
-homomorphisms / completely isometric, then so is
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
.

In the next section (Corollary
6.4
) we prove a result concerning equivariance of
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
.

Proof.

Since elements of the form
\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)
span a
w^{*}
-dense linear subspace in
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
, uniqueness of
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
is clear. Choose arbitrary implementations
U^{\M},U^{\widetilde{\M}},U^{\N},U^{\widetilde{\N}}
of the actions. By Proposition
4.3
we have

(5.1)

U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}\;(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))_{[24]}\;U^{\M}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\N}_{[34]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}

for any
m\in\M,n\in\N
. Define

\Upsilon_{1}\colon\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\ni x\mapsto x_{[24]}\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}),

which is a normal, injective
*
-homomorphism. Consider the composition

\operatorname{Ad}(U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]})\Upsilon_{1}\colon\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))\bar{\otimes}\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N,

which is again a normal, injective
*
-homomorphism. Equation (
5.1
) shows that it has the correct codomain. Next we compose with
\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{2}
, which is a well defined normal CB map. Indeed, it can be defined as the dual to the CB map
\textnormal{id}\otimes(\vartheta_{1})_{*}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes(\vartheta_{2})_{*}
on
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))_{*}\widehat{\otimes}\widetilde{\M}_{*}\widehat{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}\widehat{\otimes}\widetilde{\N}_{*}
(
[
12
, Corollary 7.1.3]
). On generators we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{2})\operatorname{Ad}(U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]})\Upsilon_{1}\bigl(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{1})\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{2})\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\widetilde{\M}}(\vartheta_{1}(m))_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\vartheta_{2}(n))_{[34]}\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))\bar{\otimes}\widetilde{\M}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\widetilde{\N}\end{split}

as
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
are assumed to be equivariant. Composing with
\operatorname{Ad}(U^{\widetilde{\M}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\widetilde{\N}}_{[34]})
gives that

\operatorname{Ad}(U^{\widetilde{\M}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}U^{\widetilde{\N}}_{[34]})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{2})\operatorname{Ad}(U^{\N*}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]})\Upsilon_{1}

defines a map

(5.2)

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\widetilde{\M}}\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\widetilde{\N}})\colon\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)\mapsto(\iota_{\widetilde{\M}}(\vartheta_{1}(m))\iota_{\widetilde{\N}}(\vartheta_{2}(n)))_{[24]}.

Thus we see that in fact we can take
(\widetilde{\M}\,\overline{\boxtimes}\,\widetilde{\N})_{[24]}
as the codomain of the above map. We define
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
by composition of (
5.2
) with the canonical isomorphism
(\widetilde{\M}\,\overline{\boxtimes}\,\widetilde{\N})_{[24]}\rightarrow\widetilde{\M}\,\overline{\boxtimes}\,\widetilde{\N}
. In this way we have defined a normal, linear map
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
with CB norm bounded above by
\|\vartheta_{1}\|_{cb}\|\vartheta_{2}\|_{cb}
(as this property holds for tensor products).

Clearly if
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}
are unital, then so is
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
. If
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}
are CP then so is
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
as all the involved maps are CP. An analogous argument works for
*
-homomorphisms and also for completely isometric maps. Indeed, by
[
12
, Corollary 4.1.9]
,
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}
are completely isometric if, and only if
(\vartheta_{1})_{*},(\vartheta_{2})_{*}
are complete quotient mappings. By
[
12
, Proposition 7.1.7]

\textnormal{id}\otimes(\vartheta_{1})_{*}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes(\vartheta_{2})_{*}
also is a complete quotient mapping, hence
\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{2}
is completely isometric.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

If
\omega_{\M}\in\M_{*},\omega_{\N}\in\N_{*}
are faithful, invariant states, then one can show that
\omega_{\M}\boxtimes\omega_{\N}
is a faithful normal state on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
. We prove this in our next work concerning standard structure for
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
(
[
11
]
).

Let us recall definitions of approximation properties for a von Neumann algebra
\operatorname{P}
:

•

\operatorname{P}
has
w^{*}
CPAP
(or is
semidiscrete
) if there exists a net
(\vartheta_{i})_{i\in I}
of unital, normal, finite rank CP maps
\operatorname{P}\rightarrow\operatorname{P}
such that
\vartheta_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
in the point-
w^{*}
-topology (see
[
4
, Definition 2.3.3]
and
[
5
, Lemma 2.1]
).

•

\operatorname{P}
has
w^{*}
CBAP
if there exists a net
(\vartheta_{i})_{i\in I}
of finite rank, normal CB maps
\operatorname{P}\rightarrow\operatorname{P}
such that
\vartheta_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
in the point-
w^{*}
-topology and
\sup_{i\in I}\|\vartheta_{i}\|_{cb}<+\infty
. The smallest bound
\Lambda_{cb}(\operatorname{P})
for this value is called the Cowling-Haagerup constant of
\operatorname{P}
(
[
4
, Definition 12.3.9]
).

•

\operatorname{P}
has
w^{*}
OAP
if there exists a net
(\vartheta_{i})_{i\in I}
of finite rank, normal CB maps
\operatorname{P}\rightarrow\operatorname{P}
such that
(\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})x\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}x
weak
∗
for all Hilbert spaces
\mathsf{H}
and
x\in\operatorname{P}\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
. In other words,
\vartheta_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
in the stable point-
w^{*}
-topology (see
[
17
, section 1,2, Proposition 1.7]
).

We can use Proposition
5.1
to show that the braided tensor product preserves the above approximation properties, provided that the maps
\vartheta_{i}
are equivariant (in Corollary
8.5
we show that we cannot completely abandon the equivariance condition). Let
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N,\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
be as above.

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume that
\M
and
\N
both simultaneously have one of the approximation properties
w^{*}
CPAP,
w^{*}
CBAP,
w^{*}
OAP and we can choose the implementing maps
\vartheta_{i}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\M)(i\in I),\psi_{j}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\N)(j\in J)
to be equivariant. Then
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
also has the relevant approximation property.

Proof.

Consider normal, CB maps
\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j}\,(i\in I,j\in J)
on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
, defined in Proposition
5.1
. They are finite rank. Indeed, for any
m\in\M,n\in\N
we have

(\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j})(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))=\iota_{\M}(\vartheta_{i}(m))\iota_{\N}(\psi_{j}(n))

hence the claim follows from the definition of
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
and the fact that finite dimensional subspaces are weak
∗
-closed (
[
40
, Theorem 1.21]
). Taking into consideration properties of
\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j}
proved in Proposition
5.1
, it is enough to check that the identity
\textnormal{id}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)
belongs to the stable point-
w^{*}
-closure of
\{\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j}\mid i\in I,j\in J\}
. Fix a Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
,
x\in(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
and
\omega\in((\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}))_{*}
.

Let
\Upsilon\colon\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))\bar{\otimes}\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N
be the injective, normal
*
-homomorphism given on generators by
\Upsilon(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n))=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}
(Proposition
4.3
). Note that for
m\in\M,n\in\N
,
T\in\B(\mathsf{H})

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)\otimes T)\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(\vartheta_{i}(m))_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}\alpha^{\N}(\psi_{j}(n))_{[34]}\otimes T\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i})\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\psi_{j})\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\otimes T\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\psi_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[34]}\otimes T\bigr)\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\psi_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)\otimes T\bigr),\end{split}

hence by continuity

(5.3)

(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j})\otimes\textnormal{id})(y)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\psi_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})(y)

for all
y\in(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
. Observe that for this calculation we needed the assumption that
\vartheta_{i},\psi_{j}
are equivariant. An analogous reasoning shows

(5.4)

(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\textnormal{id})\otimes\textnormal{id})(y)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})(y).

Next, note that
\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id}
is an embedding of
(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
into
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))\bar{\otimes}\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
, hence we can find a normal functional
\widetilde{\omega}
on
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))\bar{\otimes}\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}\N\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
such that
\omega(y)=\widetilde{\omega}(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})(y)
. Fix
i\in I
and calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;\omega\bigl(((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j})\otimes\textnormal{id})x-((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\textnormal{id})\otimes\textnormal{id})x\bigr)=\widetilde{\omega}(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j})\otimes\textnormal{id})x-((\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\textnormal{id})\otimes\textnormal{id})x\bigr)\\
&=\widetilde{\omega}\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\psi_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\,(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})x\,)-(\textnormal{id}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Upsilon\otimes\textnormal{id})x\bigr)\xrightarrow[j\in J]{}0,\end{split}

since
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\psi_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})_{j\in J}
converges to the identity in the point-
w^{*}
-topology (
[
17
, Proposition 1.7]
). This shows that
\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j}\xrightarrow[j\in J]{}\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\textnormal{id}
in the stable point-
w^{*}
topology. Similarly we check that
\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\textnormal{id}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
in the stable point-
w^{*}
-topology, hence
\textnormal{id}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)
belongs to the stable point-
w^{*}
-closure of
\{\vartheta_{i}\boxtimes\psi_{j}\mid i\in I,j\in J\}
as claimed (c.f. the proof of
[
9
, Theorem 7.16]
).
∎

6.
Action on braided tensor product and canonical quantum subgroups of
\mathbb{G}

Let
(\mathbb{G},\widehat{\operatorname{R}})
be a quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group, i.e.
\mathbb{G}
is a locally compact quantum group and
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix. Define two weak
∗
-closed subspaces

\begin{split}\operatorname{L}_{1}&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)\widehat{\operatorname{R}}\mid\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\}\subseteq\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),\\
\operatorname{L}_{2}&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}\mid\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\}\subseteq\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}).\end{split}

It is not difficult to see that
\operatorname{L}_{1},\operatorname{L}_{2}
are in fact unital
*
-subalgebras, i.e. von Neumann subalgebras of
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Then using the bicommutant theorem we see that
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}\in\operatorname{L}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}_{2}
. In fact,
\operatorname{L}_{1},\LL_{2}
are globally invariant under the unitary antipode and scaling group, and satisfy

\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\operatorname{L}_{1})\subseteq\operatorname{L}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}_{1},\quad\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\operatorname{L}_{2})\subseteq\operatorname{L}_{2}\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}_{2}

(see
[
20
, Proposition 4.1]
). This means that
\operatorname{L}_{1},\operatorname{L}_{2}
are
Baaj-Vaes subalgebras
and by
[
3
, Proposition A.5]
there exist locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
such that

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})=\operatorname{L}_{1},\quad\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})=\operatorname{L}_{2},

with comultiplications of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}
restrictions of the comultiplication of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
. Denote by

\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),\quad\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})

the formal inclusion maps; we see that they make
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
into quantum subgroups of
\mathbb{G}
. In particular, any action of
\mathbb{G}
can be restricted to
\mathbb{G}_{1}
or
\mathbb{G}_{2}
. As usual, we will denote by
\theta_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}},\theta_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}
the associated strong quantum homomorphisms.

Remark 6.1
.

In the special case when
\mathbb{G}=D(\mathbb{H})
is the Drinfeld double of a locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{H}
, we have
\mathbb{G}_{1}=\mathbb{H}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}=\widehat{\mathbb{H}}
. Indeed, this is an immediate consequence of the definition of
\operatorname{R}
-matrix:
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}=(\gamma_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{H})}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{H}\subseteq D(\mathbb{H})})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}})
(Proposition
2.7
).

We can see the unitary
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
in four different ways: as an element of
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}\!)\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}\!)
,
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}\!)\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}\!)
or
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Let us denote by
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{12},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
this operator, considered as an element of the respective algebra. By the way we define comultiplication on
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}
, each of these unitaries is then a bicharacter. We note that there are expected relations between their lifts:

(6.1)

\begin{split}(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u},\\
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1}\\
(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2}).\end{split}

These properties follow from uniqueness of lifts,
[
30
, Proposition 4.7]
. The unitaries
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}
retain a version of the
\operatorname{R}
-matrix condition.

Lemma 6.2
.

For
\widehat{x}\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}),\widehat{y}\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
we have

\begin{split}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u,op}(\widehat{x})&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}^{u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u}(\widehat{x})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}^{u*},\\
(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}}^{u,op}(\widehat{y})&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u}(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}}^{u}(\widehat{y})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u*}.\end{split}

Proof.

Since
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix and
\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}
is the inclusion map which respects coproducts, we have at the reduced level

(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{op})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{*}

or equivalently

(6.2)

\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{*}.

Applying
\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{r,u}
gives

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\\
&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{*}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u*}.\end{split}

Combining this with (
6.2
) we get

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\\
&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{*}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u*}\\
&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]},\end{split}

hence after cancelling of
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}
and cutting of the third leg we get

(6.3)

\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{r,u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{r,u*}.

Applying
\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{r,u}\otimes\textnormal{id}
we have

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[34]}^{u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[14]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[34]}^{u*}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[24]}^{r,u*}

and similarly combining this with (
6.3
) we arrive at

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{u*}

or equivalently
