(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u,op})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{u*}.

Slicing off the first leg, gives the first equation of the claim. The second one can be proved in an analogous way.
∎

If
\M,\N
are von Neumann algebras which carry (an appropriate) action, we can consider their braided tensor product defined using one of the bicharacters
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{12},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
. Proposition
4.13
tells us that we do not risk running into an ambiguity by writing simply
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
. For example, assume that
\M
carries an action of
\mathbb{G}_{1}
and
\N
an action of
\mathbb{G}
, then we can define the braided tensor product using the bicharacter
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}
. We could also restrict the action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
to
\mathbb{G}_{2}
and define
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
using
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{12}
– both constructions give us isomorphic von Neumann algebras (and actually
equal
if we choose coherent implementations). In the next proposition we introduce a canonical action on the braided tensor product (c.f.
[
32
, Proposition 4.2]
). Recall the notation
U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{H}}
introduced in Section
2
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\M,\N
be von Neumann algebras.

(1)

If
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M,\N
, then there is a unique action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
such that the embeddings of
\M,\N
are
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant. If the representations
U^{\M},U^{\N}
implement the actions on
\M,\N
, then
U^{\M}\otop U^{\N}
implements the action on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
.

(2)

If
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
, then there is a unique action
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
such that the embeddings of
\M,\N
are
\mathbb{G}_{1}
-equivariant (considering the restricted action
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\N
). If the representations
U^{\M},U^{\N}
implement the actions on
\M,\N
, then
U^{\M}\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})
implements the action on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
.

(3)

If
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\N
, then there is a unique action
\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
such that the embeddings of
\M,\N
are
\mathbb{G}_{2}
-equivariant (considering the restricted action
\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\M
). If the representations
U^{\M},U^{\N}
implement the actions on
\M,\N
, then
(U^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}})\otop U^{\N}
implements the action on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
.

Note that in cases
(1),(2),(3)
braided tensor product is constructed using respective bicharacter
\widehat{\operatorname{R}},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}
.

Proof.

Notice first that if there is an action on
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
for which the embeddings of
\M,\N
are equivariant, then it has to be unique. Consequently, it is enough to show existence of such an action, and that it is implemented by representations as in the claim. Let
U^{\M},U^{\N}
be implementations of actions on
\M,\N
.

(Case
(1)
). We claim that the map

(6.4)

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\ni z\mapsto(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes z)(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)

is well defined, and that the embeddings of
\M,\N
are equivariant; then (
6.4
) is the desired action of
\mathbb{G}
. Take
m\in\M,n\in\N
. We have

(6.5)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\iota_{\N}(n))(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})=U^{\N*}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)U^{\M}_{[12]}U^{\N}_{[13]}\\
&=U^{\N*}_{[13]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)U^{\N}_{[13]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\N})\alpha^{\N}(n)\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N).\end{split}

Next, using the universal version of (
2.7
),

(6.6)

\begin{split}&\quad\;(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\iota_{\M}(m))(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})=U^{\N*}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[23]}m_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[23]}^{*}U^{\M}_{[12]}U^{\N}_{[13]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[23]})m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{[23]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}_{[13]})\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{op})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[23]})m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{op})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}))\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}*}))m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{[23]})\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[23]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}U^{\N*}_{[13]}m_{[2]}U^{\N}_{[13]}U^{\M}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})_{[23]}^{*}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\M})\alpha^{\M}(m)\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N).\end{split}

Equations (
6.5
), (
6.6
) prove that (
6.4
) is well defined and that the embeddings
\iota_{\M},\iota_{\N}
are equivariant.

(Case
(2)
). Define the map

(6.7)

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\ni z\mapsto(U^{\M}\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}))^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes z)(U^{\M}\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}))\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N).

As in case
(1)
, we need to show that this map is well defined and that the embeddings of
\M,\N
are equivariant. Recall that the restricted action
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\N
is implemented by
U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
and
\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}
(Section
2
). A calculation analogous to (
6.5
) shows that
\iota_{\N}
is equivariant. Next, using Lemma
6.2
,

\begin{split}&\quad\;(U^{\M}\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}))^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\iota_{\M}(m))(U^{\M}\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}))\\
&=(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{*}_{[13]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})_{[23]}m_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})_{[23]}^{*}U^{\M}_{[12]}(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})((\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{op})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}*})\,\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[23]})m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[23]}^{u*}\,(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{op})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}))\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}^{u})_{[23]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}*}_{[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}*}_{[13]})m_{[2]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}_{[12]})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}^{u})_{[23]}^{*}\\
&=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}^{u})_{[23]}U^{\M*}_{[12]}m_{[2]}U^{\M}_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}^{u})_{[23]}^{*}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\M})\alpha^{\M}(m)\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N),\end{split}

which proves the claim. Case
(3)
can be proven in an analogous way.
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
\M,\widetilde{\M},\N,\widetilde{\N}
be von Neumann algebras and
\vartheta_{1}\colon\M\rightarrow\widetilde{\M},\vartheta_{2}\colon\N\rightarrow\widetilde{\N}
normal, completely bounded maps.

(1)

If
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M,\widetilde{\M},\N,\widetilde{\N}
and
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}
are
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant, then
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
is
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant.

(2)

If
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M,\widetilde{\M},\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N,\widetilde{\N}
,
\vartheta_{1}
is
\mathbb{G}_{1}
-equivariant and
\vartheta_{2}
is
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant, then
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
is
\mathbb{G}_{1}
-equivariant.

(3)

If
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M,\widetilde{\M},\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\N,\widetilde{\N}
,
\vartheta_{1}
is
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant and
\vartheta_{2}
is
\mathbb{G}_{2}
-equivariant, then
\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}
is
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant.

Proof.

We prove
(1)
, the proof of the remaining assertions being analogous. Take
x\in\M,y\in\N
. Denote by
\iota_{\M},\iota_{\N}
the embeddings into
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
and by
\iota_{\widetilde{\M}},\iota_{\widetilde{\N}}
the embeddings into
\widetilde{\M}\overline{\boxtimes}\widetilde{\N}
. The claim follows from the following calculation on generators:

\begin{split}&\quad\;\alpha^{\widetilde{\M}\overline{\boxtimes}\widetilde{\N}}(\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2})(\iota_{\M}(x)\iota_{\N}(y))=\alpha^{\widetilde{\M}\overline{\boxtimes}\widetilde{\N}}(\iota_{\widetilde{\M}}(\vartheta_{1}(x))\iota_{\widetilde{\N}}(\vartheta_{2}(y)))=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\widetilde{\M}})\alpha^{\widetilde{\M}}(\vartheta_{1}(x))(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\widetilde{\N}})\alpha^{\widetilde{\N}}(\vartheta_{2}(y))\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\widetilde{\M}}\vartheta_{1})\alpha^{\M}(x)(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\widetilde{\N}}\vartheta_{2})\alpha^{\N}(y)=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}))\bigl((\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\M})\alpha^{\M}(x)\,(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{\N})\alpha^{\N}(y)\bigr)\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\vartheta_{1}\boxtimes\vartheta_{2}))\alpha^{\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N}(\iota_{\M}(x)\iota_{\N}(y)).\end{split}

∎

The coherence property proved in Proposition
4.13
, and recalled in special case before Proposition
6.3
, has its dynamical counterpart. More precisely, assume that the quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
acts on
\M,\N
. Then part
(1)
of Proposition
6.3
gives us a canonical action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
, which can be restricted to
\mathbb{G}_{1}
or
\mathbb{G}_{2}
. We can also consider restricted actions
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M
or
\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\N
, then parts
(2)
and
(3)
of Proposition
6.3
give us respectively: an action
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
on the braided tensor product constructed using
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1}
, and an action
\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
on the braided tensor product constructed using
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

In the above situation, we have the following properties:

(1)

\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is the restriction of
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
to
\mathbb{G}_{1}
,

(2)

\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is the restriction of
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
to
\mathbb{G}_{2}
.

Proof.

Choose
U^{\M},U^{\N}
, implementations of actions
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M,\N
on some Hilbert spaces
\mathsf{H}_{\M},\mathsf{H}_{\N}
. According to Proposition
6.3
, the action
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is implemented by
(U^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})
, hence claim
(1)
follows from

\begin{split}&\quad\;(U^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})=(U^{\M}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[12]}(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[13]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u,op})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}^{u,op}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{1}})=(U^{\M}\otop U^{\N})\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}.\end{split}

Case
(2)
is analogous.
∎

Next we want to prove an associativity of braided tensor product;
\M\overline{\boxtimes}(\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P})=(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}
. The minimal situation when both sides of this equality make sense, is when
\M,\N,\operatorname{P}
are von Neumann algebras equipped with actions
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N,\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\operatorname{P}
. Then we have canonical actions
\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P},\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
and the second braided tensor product is well defined. Choose arbitrary representations and implementations of actions on
\mathsf{H}_{\M},\mathsf{H}_{\N},\mathsf{H}_{\operatorname{P}}
, then both
\M\overline{\boxtimes}(\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P})
and
(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}
are subspaces of
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\operatorname{P}})
.

Proposition 6.6
.

The von Neumann algebras
\M\overline{\boxtimes}(\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P})
and
(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}
in
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\operatorname{P}})
are equal. Furthermore the associated embeddings of
\M,\N,\operatorname{P}
are equal.

Remark 6.7
.

(1)

If
\M
carries an action of
\mathbb{G}
, then we can construct
\M\overline{\boxtimes}(\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}),(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}
also in another way; using respectively
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}
and
\widehat{\operatorname{R}},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}
. In this situation (the obvious modification of) Proposition
6.6
still holds, by Propositions
4.13
,
6.5
. A similar remark applies when
\N
carries an action of
\mathbb{G}
or both
\M,\N
are equipped with an action of
\mathbb{G}
.

(2)

When
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
(or
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\operatorname{P}
or both), then Proposition
6.3
equips the iterated braided tensor product with a canonical action of
\mathbb{G}_{2}
(or
\mathbb{G}_{1}
or
\mathbb{G}
). Since choosing different ordering of parentheses gives us the same algebra
and
the same embeddings, this action is also uniquely determined.

Proof of Proposition
6.6
.

The action
\mathbb{G}_{2}\curvearrowright\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}
is implemented by
(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}})\otop U^{\operatorname{P}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}}^{u,op})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}_{2}}
, hence
\phi_{\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}}=(\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}}^{u,op}
. Using this and (
6.1
) we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\M\overline{\boxtimes}(\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P})\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})^{*}x_{[23]}\mid m\in\M,x\in\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[12]})x_{[23]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\mid m\in\M,x\in\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[12]})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u})_{[23]}n_{[2]}(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u})^{*}_{[23]}p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{1[12]})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u})_{[23]}n_{[2]}(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{2}^{u})^{*}_{[23]}p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\end{split}

and on the other hand, since
\mathbb{G}_{1}\curvearrowright\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is implemented by
U^{\M}\otop(U^{\N}\mathord{\upharpoonright}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})
with
\phi_{\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N}=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u,op}
,

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})y_{[12]}(\phi_{\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})^{*}p_{[3]}\mid y\in\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})y_{[12]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[23]})p_{[3]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\mid y\in\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})_{[12]}m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})_{[12]}^{*}n_{[2]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[23]})p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})_{[12]}^{*}n_{[2]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{2[23]})p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}.\end{split}

Observe (using Lemma
6.2
) that we have a version of the Yang-Baxter equation (c.f. Proposition
2.3
):

\begin{split}&\quad\;\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u,op}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\\
&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[12]}^{u}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{u}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{1[12]}^{u*}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\theta}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{G}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[23]})\\
&=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]}.\end{split}

Using this twice, we further have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1})_{[12]}^{*}n_{[2]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{2[23]})p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{1[12]})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{1[12]})n_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{2[23]})p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{1[12]})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})n_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{2[23]})p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{1[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{12[13]})m_{[1]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{12[13]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{1[12]})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u}_{2[23]})n_{[2]}(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\operatorname{P}})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u*}_{2[23]})p_{[3]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N,p\in\operatorname{P}\},\end{split}

which shows equality
\M\overline{\boxtimes}(\N\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P})=(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N)\overline{\boxtimes}\operatorname{P}
. Note that our calculation shows that generators in both descriptions are equal; we have an equality of embeddings.
∎

Corollary 6.8
.

For a finite family
\M_{1},\dotsc,\M_{n}\,(n\geq 2)
of von Neumann algebras equipped with actions of
\mathbb{G}
, we can define their braided tensor product
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{n}
. It is a von Neumann algebra equipped with embeddings
\iota_{\M_{i}}\colon\M_{i}\rightarrow\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{n}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
and the unique action of
\mathbb{G}
such that
\iota_{\M_{i}}\,(1\leq i\leq n)
are equivariant.

In other words, we can define
\M_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{n}
and the rest of the structure, by putting parentheses in an arbitrary order; this can be formally proved by an induction on
n
.

7.
Infinite braided tensor product

Let
(\mathbb{G},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
) be a quasi-triangular locally compact quantum group and
(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})\,(n\in\mathbb{N})

\mathbb{G}
-von Neumann algebras together with chosen faithful, normal,
\mathbb{G}
-invariant states. In this section, we will define infinite braided tensor product
\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
. This will be a von Neumann algebra carrying a canonical action of
\mathbb{G}
, together with equivariant embeddings.

Represent
\M_{-n}
in the standard way on
\LL^{2}(\M_{-n})
and let
\Omega_{-n}
be the unique unit vector in the standard positive cone, such that
\omega_{-n}(m)=\langle\Omega_{-n}|m\Omega_{-n}\rangle
for
m\in\M_{-n}
(
[
15
, Lemma 2.10]
). Let
\mathsf{H}
be the infinite tensor product
\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n})
, see
[
46
, Section 1 XIV]
. For
N\in\mathbb{N}
, consider
\M_{-N}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}
represented on
\LL^{2}(\M_{-N})\otimes\cdots\otimes\LL^{2}(\M_{-1})
and its amplification

\operatorname{P}_{N}=\{\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\otimes x\mid x\in\M_{-N}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}\}\subseteq\B\bigl(\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n})\bigr).

The sequence
(\operatorname{P}_{N})_{N=1}^{\infty}
is increasing. Indeed, this follows directly from the definition; one of the generators of
\M_{-N-1}\overline{\boxtimes}(\M_{-N}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1})
is
\mathbbm{1}\otimes x
for
x\in\M_{-N}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}
.

Definition 7.1
.

We define the infinite braided tensor product of the family
(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})\,(n\in\mathbb{N})
as

\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})=\overline{\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}\{\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\otimes x\mid x\in\M_{-N}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}\}}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\subseteq\B\bigl(\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n})\bigr).

Next, for
1\leq k\leq K
we define embeddings

(7.1)

\iota_{-k}\colon\M_{-k}\ni x\mapsto\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\otimes\iota_{\M_{-k}}^{\M_{-K}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}(x)\in\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})

where
\iota_{\M_{-k}}^{\M_{-K}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}
is the canonical embedding of
\M_{-k}
into
\M_{-K}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}
(see Corollary
6.8
).

Remark 7.2
.

(1)

If the action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M_{-n}
is trivial for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
coincides with the usual infinite tensor product, compare Example
8.1
(the same is true, if
\mathbb{G}
is classical and
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}=\mathbbm{1}
). Consequently, the structure of the von Neumann algebra
\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
depends on the choice of states
\omega_{-n}
(
[
46
, Section XIV]
).

(2)

The definition of the infinite braided tensor product depends on the order of the algebras, and the order structure of
-\mathbb{N}
; this shouldn’t come as a surprise in light of Example
8.6
.

(3)

In the algebraic context, a version of infinite braided tensor product was considered in
[
28
]
.

Lemma 7.3
.

The embeddings
\iota_{-k}\,(k\in\mathbb{N})
are well defined.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq k\leq K
. We want to show that
\iota_{-k}
does not depend on
K
. This is an easy consequence of Corollary
6.8
, as follows:

\iota_{\M_{-k}}^{\M_{-K}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}(x)=\iota_{\M_{-k}}^{(\M_{-K}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-k-1}\!)\,\overline{\boxtimes}(\M_{-k}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1})}(x)=\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes(K-k)}\otimes\iota_{\M_{-k}}^{\M_{-k}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}(x)

for
x\in\M_{-k}
.
∎

Proposition 7.4
.

There exists a unique action of
\mathbb{G}
on
\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
which makes the embeddings
\iota_{-k}\,(k\in\mathbb{N})
equivariant.

Before we prove this proposition, we need a lemma concerning standard implementations. We can be more general: let
\alpha^{\N}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\N
be an action of a locally compact quantum group on a von Neumann algebra,
\rho
strictly positive, self-adjoint operator affiliated with
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
and
\omega
an n.s.f. weight on
\N
which is
\rho
-invariant (see
[
47
, Definition 2.3]
). Assume furthermore
\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}(\rho)=\rho\otimes\rho
, or equivalently
\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}(\rho^{it})=\rho^{it}\otimes\rho^{it}\,(t\in\mathbb{R})
. Represent
\N
on its standard Hilbert space
\LL^{2}(\N)
, identified with the GNS Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}_{\omega}
for
\omega
. Let
U^{\N}
be the
standard
implementation of action, i.e. the one introduced in
3
3

3

More precisely, because we want
U^{\N}
to be a representation of
\mathbb{H}
, we take the adjoint of the unitary from
[
47
]
.

[
47
, Definition 3.6]
).

Lemma 7.5
.

For
n\in\mathfrak{N}_{\omega}
and
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi}
such that
\Lambda_{\varphi}(x)\in\Dom(\rho^{1/2})
we have
\alpha^{\N}(n)(y\otimes\mathbbm{1})\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi\otimes\omega}
and

(7.2)

U^{\N*}(\rho^{1/2}\Lambda_{\varphi}(x)\otimes\Lambda_{\omega}(n))=\Lambda_{\varphi\otimes\omega}(\alpha^{\N}(n)(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})).

In particular, if
\omega
is a
\mathbb{G}
-invariant faithful normal state on
\N
and
\Omega_{\N}\in\mathsf{H}_{\omega}
is the unit vector in the standard positive cone which implements
\omega
, then
U^{N}(\xi\otimes\Omega_{\N})=\xi\otimes\Omega_{\N}
for
\xi\in\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})
.

Proof.

The first part can be proved by first establishing the existence of a unitary representation satisfying (
7.2
) along the lines of
[
13
, Théorème 2.9]
, and then by reasoning analogous to
[
47
, Proposition 4.3]
(which is exactly our claim for
\rho=\delta^{-1}
). For the second part, if
\rho=\mathbbm{1}
and
\omega=\omega_{\Omega_{\N}}
, then for
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi}
we have

U^{\N*}(\Lambda_{\varphi}(x)\otimes\Omega_{\N})=U^{\N*}(\Lambda_{\varphi}(x)\otimes\Lambda_{\omega}(\mathbbm{1}))=\Lambda_{\varphi\otimes\omega}(\alpha^{\N}(\mathbbm{1})(x\otimes\mathbbm{1}))=\Lambda_{\varphi}(x)\otimes\Omega_{\N},

and the claim follows by approximation.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.4
.

The images of the maps
\iota_{-k}(k\in\mathbb{N})
generate the whole von Neumann algebra, hence uniqueness of the action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
is clear. We need to show its existence. In the rest of the proof, legs of
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\B(\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n}))
will be numbered by
1,\dotsc,-3,-2,-1
. Let
U^{\M_{-n}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}))
be the standard implementation of the action
\alpha^{\M_{-n}}
. We claim that the sequence of unitaries
(U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]})_{N=1}^{\infty}
converges in
\textsc{sot}^{*}
to a unitary
U
. Recall that the unitary group is
sot
∗
-complete (
[
44
, Remark 4.10]
), thus it is enough to show that for any vector
\Theta\in\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes(\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n}))
, the sequences
\bigl(U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\Theta\bigr)_{N=1}^{\infty}
and
\bigl(U^{\M_{-1}*}_{[1,-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-N}*}_{[1,-N]}\Theta\bigr)_{N=1}^{\infty}
are Cauchy. Since we are dealing with bounded sequences, it is enough to consider vectors in a linearly dense subset, so take
\Theta=\xi\otimes(\cdots\otimes\Omega_{-k-1}\otimes\zeta)
for some
\xi\in\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}),k\in\mathbb{N},\zeta\in\LL^{2}(\M_{-k})\otimes\cdots\otimes\LL^{2}(\M_{-1})
. In this case, both sequences are eventually constant. Indeed, for
N>k
we have using Lemma
7.5

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\Theta=U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-k-1}}_{[1,-k-1]}\bigl(U^{\M_{-k}}_{[1,-k]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}(\xi\otimes(\cdots\otimes\Omega_{-k-1}\otimes\zeta))\bigr)\\
&=U^{\M_{-k}}_{[1,-k]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}(\xi\otimes(\cdots\otimes\Omega_{-k-1}\otimes\zeta)).\end{split}

In particular, this shows that
\bigl(U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\Theta\bigr)_{N=1}^{\infty}
is Cauchy. Similarly, using again Lemma
7.5

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{\M_{-1}*}_{[1,-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-N}*}_{[1,-N]}\Theta=U^{\M_{-1}*}_{[1,-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-k}*}_{[1,-k]}\bigl(U^{\M_{-k-1}*}_{[1,-k-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-N}*}_{[1,-N]}(\xi\otimes(\cdots\otimes\Omega_{-k-1}\otimes\zeta))\bigr)\\
&=U^{\M_{-1}*}_{[1,-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-k}*}_{[1,-k]}(\xi\otimes(\cdots\otimes\Omega_{-k-1}\otimes\zeta)).\end{split}

We can conclude that there is a unitary
U
such that
U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}U
in
sot
∗
. Using the bicommutant theorem, we easily see that
U\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\B(\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n}))
. Just as the tensor product of representations is a representation, we see that
U
is a representation. Recall that multiplication on bounded sets is jointly continuous in
sot
. Take
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\M_{-k}
. Using Corollary
6.8
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\iota_{-k}(x))U=\underset{N\to\infty}{\textsc{sot}{-}\lim}\,U^{\M_{-1}*}_{[1,-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-N}*}_{[1,-N]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\iota_{-k}(x))U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\\
&=U^{\M_{-1}*}_{[1,-1]}\cdots U^{\M_{-k}*}_{[1,-k]}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\otimes\iota_{-k}^{\M_{-k}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}(x))U^{\M_{-k}}_{[1,-k]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\\
&=\alpha^{\M_{-k}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}\bigl(\iota_{-k}^{\M_{-k}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}}(x)\bigr)_{[1,-k,\dotsc,-1]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{-k}^{\M_{-k}\overline{\boxtimes}\cdots\overline{\boxtimes}\M_{-1}})(\alpha^{\M_{-k}}(x))_{[1,-k,\dotsc,-1]}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota_{-k})(\alpha^{\M_{-k}}(x)).\end{split}

This shows that the map

\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})\ni z\mapsto U^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes z)U\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\bigl(\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})\bigr)

is well defined, and defines an action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
for which the embeddings
\iota_{-k}(k\in\mathbb{N})
are equivariant.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

(1)

It follows from the proof of Proposition
7.4
, that if
U^{\M_{-n}}
are the standard implementations of actions
\alpha^{\M_{-n}}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M_{-n}
, then the action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=\infty}^{1}(\M_{-n},\omega_{-n})
is implemented by the unitary representation

(7.3)

\underset{N\to\infty}{\textsc{sot}^{*}{-}\lim}\,U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\B\bigl(\otimes_{n=\infty}^{1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{-n}),\Omega_{-n})\bigr).

(2)

If
\mathbb{G}
is a classical locally compact group, we can take
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}=\mathbbm{1}
as the
\operatorname{R}
-matrix. In this case Proposition
7.4
recovers the classical notion of the diagonal action on the infinite tensor product.

8.
Examples

In this section, we present several (classes of) examples of braided tensor products. First, we briefly discuss trivial cases, where the braided tensor product is easily seen to be equal to the usual tensor product. Next, less trivially, we obtain the same conclusion if one of the actions is inner. Next we show that construction of Houdayer
[
19
]
can be realised as a braided tensor product. In the fourth example, we present a construction of a braided tensor product
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
and normal functionals
\omega,\nu
for which one cannot define functional “
\omega\boxtimes\nu
”. In the last subsection we show how one can realise the crossed product as a braided tensor product, and deduce from this several interesting phenomena.

8.1.
Example 1

Let
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
be locally compact quantum groups acting on von Neumann algebras, and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
a bicharacter. Recall that to construct the braided tensor product, we choose arbitrary representations
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}),\N\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\N})
and implementations
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}},U^{\N}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
of these actions. Then

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N}),

which is a von Neumann algebra independent (up to canonical isomorphism) of the above choices. If the action
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M
is trivial, then we can take
\phi_{\M}
to be the counit, and by Remark
2.2
we obtain
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N=\M\bar{\otimes}\N
. Similarly, if the action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
is trivial, then
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N=\M\bar{\otimes}\N
. The same conclusion holds for
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=\mathbbm{1}
and arbitrary actions.

8.2.
Example 2

Let
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M,\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
be locally compact quantum groups acting on von Neumann algebras, and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
a bicharacter. Assume that the action
\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\N
is inner. More precisely, let
U^{\N}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}}
be a representation s.t.
U^{\N}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\N
and
\alpha^{\N}(n)=U^{\N*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)U^{\N}
is an action of
\mathbb{G}
on
\N
. In other words, the action
\alpha^{\N}
is
cocycle equivalent
to the trivial action (
[
47
, Definition 1.5]
). For example, we can take
U^{\N}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}
and
\N=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
or
\N=\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
. Represent
\N
on
\mathsf{H}_{\N}
,
\M
on
\mathsf{H}_{\M}
and let
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{H}}
be an implementation of the action
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M
.

Proposition 8.1
.

In this situation we have
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N=\M\bar{\otimes}\N
, as an equality of subsets of
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{\N})
.

Proof.

Recall that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u}=(\Phi\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\Phi}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}
(Lemma
2.5
). First we perform an auxilliary calculation

\begin{split}\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\Phi})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}})(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N}\widehat{\Phi})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{\bigl((\phi_{\N}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}*})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\phi_{\N}\widehat{\Phi}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}})(n\otimes\mathbbm{1})\bigr)_{[21]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{\bigl((\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M,u}(m)(n\otimes\mathbbm{1})\bigr)_{[21]}\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}.\end{split}

Note that
\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**}\rightarrow\N
, hence using the universal version of the Podleś condition (Proposition
4.8
) we can continue

\begin{split}\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{\bigl((\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})\alpha^{\M,u}(m)(\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}(x)n\otimes\mathbbm{1})\bigr)_{[21]}\mid m\in\M,x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**},n\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{\bigl((\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\alpha^{\M,u}(m)(x\otimes\mathbbm{1}))\,(n\otimes\mathbbm{1})\bigr)_{[21]}\mid m\in\M,x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**},n\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{\bigl((\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}\otimes\textnormal{id})(x\otimes m)\,(n\otimes\mathbbm{1})\bigr)_{[21]}\mid m\in\M,x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**},n\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{m\otimes\phi_{\N}^{\vN}\widehat{\Phi}^{\vN}(x)n\mid m\in\M,x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})^{**},n\in\N\}=\M\bar{\otimes}\N.\end{split}

∎

By Proposition
4.14
, a claim similar to Proposition
8.1
holds if
\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\M
is inner.

8.3.
Example 3

Let
\M,\N
be von Neumann algebras equipped with actions
\alpha^{\M}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{R}}\curvearrowright\M
,
\alpha^{\N}\colon\mathbb{R}\curvearrowright\N
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{R}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{R}})
be the Kac-Takesaki operator. Then
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
can be identified with the construction of Houdayer
[
19
, Definition 1.1]
, called the crossed product of
\M
and
\N
. Indeed, to see this, represent
\M
and
\alpha^{\M}
in an arbitrary way. Next, represent
\N
on
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R})\bar{\otimes}\N
via the (injective) normal map
\alpha^{\N}
, and take for the implementation of the action
\alpha^{\N}
the unitary
\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\pi_{\widehat{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}))(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{R}})
. Then for
m\in\M,n\in\N
we have

\begin{split}\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{R}})_{[12]}(m\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{R}})_{[12]}^{*}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[23]}=\alpha^{\M}(m)_{[21]}\alpha^{\N}(n)_{[23]}.\end{split}

Hence we see that, up to passing between left and right actions,
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is isomorphic with the crossed product of Houdayer. This construction was used in
[
19
]
to produce an uncountable family of pairwise non-isomorphic type
\operatorname{III}_{1}
factors which are non-full, and have the same
\tau
invariant
[
19
, Theorem 3.9]
.

8.4.
Example 4

In this example we use infinite (braided) tensor products to show that even though the braided tensor product “
\omega\boxtimes\nu
” of equivariant functionals is always well defined (Proposition
5.1
), it might not exist without the equivariance assumption (see Proposition
8.3
).

Let us identify
\widehat{\mathbb{R}}
with
\mathbb{R}
via the pairing
\langle\xi,x\rangle=e^{2\pi i\xi x}
. For
a\in\mathbb{R}
, let
(\mathbb{R},\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a})
be a quasi-triangular (classical) locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{R}
with an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}(\xi,\eta)=e^{2\pi ia\xi\eta}\,(\xi,\eta\in\widehat{\mathbb{R}})
. For
n\in-\mathbb{N}
, consider
\M_{n}=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
, for
\mathbb{T}\subseteq\mathbb{C}
the unit circle, with the usual faithful normal state
\omega_{n}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}
given by integration with respect to the normalised Lebesgue measure. Fix the action
\alpha^{\M_{n}}\colon\mathbb{R}\curvearrowright\M_{n}
given by rotation, i.e.
\alpha^{\M_{n}}(f)(x,\lambda)=f(\lambda e^{2\pi ix})
for
x\in\mathbb{R},\lambda\in\mathbb{T},f\in\M_{n}=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
, and observe that the state
\omega_{n}
is invariant. Let
U^{\M_{n}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{T}))
be the standard implementation and
\Omega_{n}=\mathbbm{1}\in\LL^{2}(\mathbb{T})
the positive unit vector which implements
\omega_{n}
.

We have a “diagonal” action of
\mathbb{R}
on the (non- or trivially- braided) infinite tensor product
\M=\bar{\otimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\M_{n},\omega_{n})
(see
[
46
, Definition 1.6 XIV]
). Indeed, take
a=0
, then
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{0}=\mathbbm{1}
and
\M
is equal to the infinite braided tensor product
\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\M_{n},\omega_{n})
and the canonical embeddings
\M_{k}\hookrightarrow\overline{\boxtimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\M_{n},\omega_{n})
are the usual embeddings onto
k
’th tensor factor (Definition
7.1
, Remark
7.6
). Proposition
7.4
gives us an action
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{R}\curvearrowright\M
for which embeddings are equivariant; this is the diagonal action. Furthermore, by Remark
7.6
we know that
\alpha^{\M}
is implemented by representation

U^{\M}=\underset{N\to\infty}{\textsc{sot}^{*}{-}\lim}\,U^{\M_{-N}}_{[1,-N]}\cdots U^{\M_{-1}}_{[1,-1]}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\bar{\otimes}\B\bigl(\otimes_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\LL^{2}(\M_{n}),\Omega_{n})\bigr).

Let
\N=\M=\bar{\otimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\N_{n},\nu_{n})
be another copy of
\bar{\otimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}),\int_{\mathbb{T}})
with the same diagonal action of
\mathbb{R}
implemented by
U^{\M}
.

Fix now
a\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Q}
. We will be interested in the braided tensor product
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\M
, constructed using the above structure. Let us denote by
\iota^{1},\iota^{2}
the canonical embeddings of
\M
into
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\M
. Since
a
is irrational, we can choose
k_{1}=0
and
k_{2},k_{3},\dotsc\in\mathbb{N}
such that the numbers
e^{2\pi iak_{n}}(n\in\mathbb{N})
are pairwise distinct. For
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
0<\delta_{N}\leq\tfrac{1}{2N}
be small enough so that the arc
\Omega_{N}=\{e^{2\pi is}\mid-\delta_{N}\leq s\leq\delta_{N}\}\subseteq\mathbb{T}
satisfies

(8.1)

e^{-2\pi iak_{n}}\Omega_{N}\cap e^{-2\pi iak_{n^{\prime}}}\Omega_{N}=\emptyset\quad(1\leq n\neq n^{\prime}\leq N).

Set
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
to be the identity function, define
g_{N}=\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}}
and for
N\in\mathbb{N},K\in\!-\mathbb{N}

(8.2)

F_{N,K}=\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\otimes 1_{\Omega_{N}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes(|K|-1)}\in\M,\quad G_{N,K}=\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\infty}\otimes g_{N}\otimes\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes(|K|-1)}\in\M.

Lemma 8.2
.

For every
N\in\mathbb{N},K\in\!-\mathbb{N}
, we have
\|F_{N,K}\|=\|1_{\Omega_{N}}\|=\|G_{N,K}\|=\|g_{N}\|=1
,
\omega_{K}(1_{\Omega_{N}})\in\left[0,\tfrac{1}{N}\right]
,
\omega_{K}(g_{N})=\tfrac{1}{N}
and
\|\iota^{1}(F_{N,K})\iota^{2}(G_{N,K})\|\leq\tfrac{1}{N}
.

Proof.

The first claim is straightforward:
\|F_{N,K}\|=\|1_{\Omega_{N}}\|=1
and
\|G_{N,K}\|=\|g_{N}\|=1
follows from evaluation at
1
. Next,
\omega_{K}(1_{\Omega_{N}})\leq\tfrac{1}{N}
follows from
\delta_{N}\leq\tfrac{1}{2N}
and

\omega_{K}(g_{N})=\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\lambda^{k_{n}}\operatorname{d}\!\lambda=\tfrac{1}{N}.

To calculate the norm of
\iota^{1}(F_{N,K})\iota^{2}(G_{N,K})
it will be most convenient to use Proposition
4.3
. Denote by
\iota^{1}_{k}
the embedding of
\M_{k}=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
onto the
k
’th tensor factor, for the first copy of
\M=\bar{\otimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}),\omega_{n})
and similarly
\iota^{2}_{k}
for the second copy of
\M
. We have

\|\iota^{1}(F_{N,K})\iota^{2}(G_{N,K})\|=\|\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}\alpha^{\M}(F_{N,K})_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\M}(G_{N,K})_{[34]}\|,

where the second norm is calculated in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R}))\bar{\otimes}\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R}))\bar{\otimes}\M
. Since
\alpha^{\M}(F_{N,K})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota^{1}_{K})\alpha^{\M_{K}}(1_{\Omega_{N}})\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\bar{\otimes}(\bar{\otimes}_{n=-\infty}^{-1}(\M_{n},\omega_{n}))
has non-trivial factors only on legs
1
,
K
(and
\mathbbm{1}
on the remaining legs), and similarly
\alpha^{\M}(G_{N,K})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\iota^{2}_{K})\alpha^{\M_{K}}(g_{N})
, we have in fact

(8.3)

\begin{split}\|\iota^{1}(F_{N,K})\iota^{2}(G_{N,K})\|&=\|\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(1_{\Omega_{N}})_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(g_{N})_{[34]}\|\\
&=\|\alpha^{\M_{K}}(1_{\Omega_{N}})_{[12]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(g_{N})_{[34]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}\|,\end{split}

with norm calculated in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R}))\bar{\otimes}\M_{K}\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R}))\bar{\otimes}\M_{K}
. Next we identify
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(g_{N})_{[34]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}
. To do this, let us introduce the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{R}}
defined via
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{R}}(f)(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{\langle\xi,x\rangle}f(x)\operatorname{d}\!x
, which is a unitary identification between
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{R}})
(in abstract setting, it is taken as the identity). Under this correspondence,
\lambda_{x}^{\mathbb{R}}\in\operatorname{vN}(\mathbb{R})
is mapped to
\overline{\langle\cdot,x\rangle}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{R}})
and
\langle\xi,\cdot\rangle=e^{2\pi i\xi\bullet}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
to
\lambda^{\widehat{\mathbb{R}}}_{\xi}\in\operatorname{vN}(\widehat{\mathbb{R}})

[
14
, Page 102]
(we write additional superscripts to avoid confusion). Using this identification we see that

\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\langle\xi,\cdot\rangle)\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}\simeq\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\lambda^{\widehat{\mathbb{R}}}_{\xi})\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}=\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}^{*}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a}(\cdot,-\xi+\cdot)(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\lambda_{\xi}^{\widehat{\mathbb{R}}})=\langle\cdot,-a\xi\rangle\otimes\lambda_{\xi}^{\widehat{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq\lambda_{a\xi}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\langle\xi,\cdot\rangle\in\operatorname{vN}(\mathbb{R})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, hence as
\alpha^{\M_{K}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=\langle 1,\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\quad\;\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(g_{N})_{[34]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}=\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[34]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}^{*}(\langle k_{n},\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[34]}\widehat{\operatorname{R}}_{a[13]}=\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\lambda_{ak_{n}}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\langle k_{n},\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[134]}.\end{split}

We can plug this into (
8.3
):

\begin{split}&\quad\;\|\iota^{1}(F_{N,K})\iota^{2}(G_{N,K})\|=\bigl\|\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(1_{\Omega_{N}})_{[12]}(\lambda_{ak_{n}}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\langle k_{n},\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[134]}\bigr\|.\end{split}

To calculate this norm, take any
h\in\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{T})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{T})
with norm
1
. Using (
8.1
), we can bound

\begin{split}&\quad\;\bigl\|\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(1_{\Omega_{N}})_{[12]}(\lambda_{ak_{n}}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\langle k_{n},\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[134]}(h)\bigr\|^{2}\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigl|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\alpha^{\M_{K}}(1_{\Omega_{N}})_{[12]}(\lambda_{ak_{n}}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\langle k_{n},\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[134]}(h)\bigr|^{2}(x,\lambda,y,\rho)\operatorname{d}\!\rho\operatorname{d}\!y\operatorname{d}\!\lambda\operatorname{d}\!x\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigl|\sum_{n=1}^{N}1_{\Omega_{\N}}(\lambda e^{2\pi ix})(\lambda_{ak_{n}}^{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\langle k_{n},\cdot\rangle\otimes\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{k_{n}})_{[134]}(h)(x,\lambda,y,\rho)\bigr|^{2}\operatorname{d}\!\rho\operatorname{d}\!y\operatorname{d}\!\lambda\operatorname{d}\!x\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigl|\sum_{n=1}^{N}1_{\Omega_{\N}}(\lambda e^{2\pi ix})\langle k_{n},y\rangle\rho^{k_{n}}h(x-ak_{n},\lambda,y,\rho)\bigr|^{2}\operatorname{d}\!\rho\operatorname{d}\!y\operatorname{d}\!\lambda\operatorname{d}\!x\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigl|\sum_{n=1}^{N}1_{\Omega_{\N}}(\lambda e^{2\pi i(x+ak_{n})})\langle k_{n},y\rangle\rho^{k_{n}}h(x,\lambda,y,\rho)\bigr|^{2}\operatorname{d}\!\rho\operatorname{d}\!y\operatorname{d}\!\lambda\operatorname{d}\!x\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}1_{e^{-2\pi iak_{n}}\Omega_{\N}}(\lambda e^{2\pi ix})\bigl|\langle k_{n},y\rangle\rho^{k_{n}}h(x,\lambda,y,\rho)\bigr|^{2}\operatorname{d}\!\rho\operatorname{d}\!y\operatorname{d}\!\lambda\operatorname{d}\!x\\
&=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}1_{\bigcup_{n=1}^{N}e^{-2\pi iak_{n}}\Omega_{\N}}(\lambda e^{2\pi ix})|h(x,\lambda,y,\rho)|^{2}\operatorname{d}\!\rho\operatorname{d}\!y\operatorname{d}\!\lambda\operatorname{d}\!x\leq\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}\|h\|_{2}^{2}=\tfrac{1}{N^{2}}.\end{split}

This ends the proof.
∎

With this lemma in hand, we can prove the following interesting property of
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\M
; recall that
\iota^{1},\iota^{2}
denote the canonical embeddings
\M\rightarrow\M\overline{\boxtimes}\M
.

Proposition 8.3
.

There are bounded, normal functionals
\rho,\nu\in\M_{*}
such that there is no
\kappa\in(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\M)^{*}
with
\kappa(\iota^{1}(x)\iota^{2}(y))=\rho(x)\nu(y)
for
x,y\in\M
.

Proof.

For
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
F_{5^{N},-N},G_{5^{N},-N}
be the functions defined in (
8.2
). Since
\|1_{\Omega_{5^{N}}}\|=\|g_{5^{N}}\|=1
, we can find states
\rho_{-N},\nu_{-N}\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{T})
such that
\rho_{-N}(1_{\Omega_{5^{N}}})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}
,
\nu_{-N}(g_{5^{N}})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}
. Define functionals
\rho,\nu
via norm convergent series

\rho=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-1}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\cdots\otimes\omega_{n-1}\otimes\rho_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\rho_{-1}\in\M_{*},\quad\nu=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-1}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\cdots\otimes\omega_{n-1}\otimes\nu_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\nu_{-1}\in\M_{*}

and assume by contradiction that there is
\kappa\in(\M\overline{\boxtimes}\M)^{*}
as in the claim (note that we do not assume that
\kappa
is normal). Then using Lemma
8.2

(8.4)

\bigl|\kappa\bigl(\iota^{1}(F_{5^{N},-N})\iota^{2}(G_{5^{N},-N})\bigr)\bigr|\leq\|\kappa\|\|\iota^{1}(F_{5^{N},-N})\iota^{2}(G_{5^{N},-N})\|\leq\tfrac{\|\kappa\|}{5^{N}}

and on the other hand, as
\rho_{n},\omega_{n},\nu_{n}
are states, using again Lemma
8.2

\begin{split}&\quad\;\kappa\bigl(\iota^{1}(F_{5^{N},-N})\iota^{2}(G_{5^{N},-N})\bigr)=\rho(F_{5^{N},-N})\nu(G_{5^{N},-N})\\
&=\bigl(\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-N}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\rho_{-N}(1_{\Omega_{5^{N}}})+\sum_{n=-N+1}^{-1}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\omega_{-N}(1_{\Omega_{5^{N}}})\bigr)\,\bigl(\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-N}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\nu_{-N}(g_{N})+\sum_{n=-N+1}^{-1}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\omega_{-N}(g_{N})\bigr)\\
&\geq\bigl(\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-N}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\rho_{-N}(1_{\Omega_{5^{N}}})\bigr)\,\bigl(\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-N}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\nu_{-N}(g_{N})\bigr)\geq\bigl(\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-N}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\tfrac{1}{2}\bigr)\,\bigl(\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-N}\tfrac{1}{2^{|n|}}\tfrac{1}{2}\bigr)=\tfrac{1}{4^{N}},\end{split}

which contradicts (
8.4
) and
\|\kappa\|<+\infty
.
∎

8.5.
Example 5

In this subsection we show how one can realise crossed products as braided tensor products.

Let
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\M})
be a von Neumann algebra with an action
\alpha^{\M}\colon\mathbb{G}\curvearrowright\M
of a locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
, and let
U^{\M}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\mathbb{G}})
be an implementation. Consider the bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. On
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
we have a translation action
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\curvearrowright\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, hence we can consider the braided tensor product
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
defined using this structure.

Proposition 8.4
.

The von Neumann algebras
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
and
\mathbb{G}\ltimes\M
are unitarily equivalent.

Proof.

Note that the action
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is implemented by
\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\pi_{\mathbb{G}})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
. By definition

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)\mid\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),m\in\M\}

and conjugating by the unitary
(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})
gives

\begin{split}&\quad\;\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}*})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)(\pi_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\phi_{\M})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}})\mid\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})U^{\M*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes m)U^{\M}\mid\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),m\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\alpha^{\M}(m)\mid\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),m\in\M\}=\mathbb{G}\ltimes\M.\end{split}

∎

As a consequence, we obtain several concrete examples with interesting behaviour.

Corollary 8.5
.

There exist braided tensor products of the form
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
with the following properties:

(1)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is of type II,
\M
is of type I and
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
is of type I,

(2)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is of type III,
\M
is of type I and
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
is of type I,

(3)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
and
\M
have
w^{*}
CBAP but
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
doesn’t have
w^{*}
CBAP,

(4)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
and
\M
are factors, but
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\overline{\boxtimes}\M
has diffuse center, in particular is not a factor.

Note that such examples don’t exist for the usual tensor product.

Proof.

It is well known, that if
\mathbb{G}
acts on
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
with respect to the translation action
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}
, then
\mathbb{G}\ltimes\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is isomorphic with the type I factor
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
. This proves existence of examples (1) and (2), when we take for
\mathbb{G}
an appropriate classical group. Concretely, for type
\operatorname{II}_{1}
take the free group on two generators
F_{2}
, for
\operatorname{II}_{\infty}
the product group
F_{2}\times(ax+b)
, where
(ax+b)
is the “
ax+b
” group (see
[
14
, Page 260]
), and for types
\operatorname{III}_{\lambda}\,(0\leq\lambda\leq 1)
e.g. the examples constructed in
[
43
]
(in the case of types
\operatorname{II}_{\infty}
,
\operatorname{III}_{\lambda}
one can take here also
discrete, quantum
examples, see
[
24
, Proposition 4.8]
and
[
23
, Corollary 4.4, Theorem 4.5]
).

It is known that although
\operatorname{SL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
are weakly amenable with Cowling-Haagerup constant equal
1
, their semidirect product is not weakly amenable (
[
16
]
). By
[
43
, Proposition 2.2]
we know that the group von Neumann algebra of a semidirect product of groups is isomorphic with the respective crossed product. For discrete groups, the Cowling-Haagerup constant is equal to the constant of its group von Neumann algebra (
[
16
, Theorem 2.6]
), which shows existence of example (3). Existence of example (4) follows from a work of Vaes (
[
48
, Proposition B]
), who constructed a crossed product
G\ltimes\B(\mathsf{H})
with diffuse center, for a Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
and a locally compact group
G
such that
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{G})=\operatorname{vN}(G)
is a factor.
∎

8.6.
Example 5

Let
\mathbb{T}
be the circle group and
D(\mathbb{T})=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{Z}
its Drinfeld double, which is a quasi-triangular quantum group with
\operatorname{R}
-matrix
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}=(\gamma_{\mathbb{Z}\subseteq D(\mathbb{T})}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{T}\subseteq D(\mathbb{T})})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}}_{[23]}
(Proposition
2.7
). Set
\M=\N=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
, fix
\zeta\in\mathbb{T}
and equip
\M,\N
with actions

\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{T}}\colon\mathbb{T}\curvearrowright\M\colon\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{T}}=\Delta_{\mathbb{T}},\quad\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{Z}}\colon\mathbb{Z}\curvearrowright\N\colon\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{Z}}(f)(k,\lambda)=f(\zeta^{k}\lambda)

and let
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{Z}}\colon\mathbb{Z}\curvearrowright\M
,
\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{T}}\colon\mathbb{T}\curvearrowright\N
be the trivial actions. Since
\mathbb{T},\mathbb{Z}
are classical, the Yetter-Drinfeld condition is met and by Proposition
2.9
we obtain actions
\alpha^{\M},\alpha^{\N}
of
D(\mathbb{T})
. For the implementation of the action
\alpha^{\M}_{\mathbb{T}}
we can take the identity, and for the implementation of the trivial actions the respective counits. We let
U^{\N,\mathbb{Z}},\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}}
be the standard implementation of
\alpha^{\N}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. By Proposition
2.10
we obtain implementations
\phi_{\M},\phi_{\N}
of the actions of
D(\mathbb{T})
. In this situation, we can consider two braided tensor products
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N,\N\overline{\boxtimes}\M
, both constructed with respect to
\widehat{\operatorname{R}}
.

Proposition 8.6
.

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N
is equal to the tensor product
\M\bar{\otimes}\N=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
, while
\N\overline{\boxtimes}\M
is isomorphic with
\mathbb{Z}\ltimes\N
. In particular, if
\zeta=e^{2\pi ix}
with
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Q}
, then
\N\overline{\boxtimes}\M
is isomorphic with the injective
\operatorname{II}_{1}
factor and
\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N\nsimeq\N\overline{\boxtimes}\M
.

Compare this result with Proposition
4.14
.

Proof.

Since all the involved quantum groups are coamenable, we have

\begin{split}\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})(f\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes g)\mid f\in\M,g\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{T}}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}}_{[23]})(f\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{T}}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}}_{[23]})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes g)\mid f\in\M,g\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{f\otimes g\mid f\in\M,g\in\N\}=\M\bar{\otimes}\N.\end{split}

If we take the braided tensor product with swapped factors, we get

\begin{split}\N\overline{\boxtimes}\M&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})(g\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\N}\otimes\phi_{\M})(\widehat{\operatorname{R}}^{u})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes f)\mid g\in\N,f\in\M\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{T}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}}_{[23]})(f\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\varepsilon_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{T}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}}_{[23]})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes g)\mid f\in\M,g\in\N\}\\
&=\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}})(f\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{T}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes g)\mid f\in\M,g\in\N\}.\end{split}

If we apply the flip, we get the isomorphic von Neumann algebra

(8.5)

\overline{\lin}^{\,\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\{(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{Z}})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes f)(\textnormal{id}\otimes\phi_{\N,\mathbb{Z}})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{Z}})(g\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mid f\in\M,g\in\N\}.

Since
\M=\N=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})
, we recognise in (
8.5
) the crossed product
\mathbb{Z}\ltimes\N
.

Assume now that
\zeta=e^{2\pi ix}
with
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Q}
. Then it is well known that the action
\mathbb{Z}\curvearrowright\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
(called the irrational rotation) is free and ergodic, hence
\mathbb{Z}\ltimes\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
is a factor
[
46
, Corollary XIII 1.6]
. It is type
\operatorname{II}_{1}
by
[
46
, Theorem XIII 1.7]
and injective by
[
46
, Theorem XV 3.16]
.
∎

9.
Appendix

9.1.
Implementation of an isomorphism

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{H}
be isomorphic locally compact quantum groups. As explained in subsection
4.2
, this means that we have compatible isomorphisms
\theta\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H})
,
\theta_{r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{H})
,
\gamma\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
which respect to the comultiplications. By taking the dual morphism, we get isomorphisms
\widehat{\theta}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,
\widehat{\theta}_{r}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,
\widehat{\gamma}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Since our von Neumann algebras
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
,
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H}))
are in standard position, by
[
45
, Theorem IX 1.14]
there is a unique unitary
v\colon\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})
which satisfies
J_{\mathbb{H}}v=vJ_{\mathbb{G}},v\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}=\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}
and implements
\gamma
in the sense that
\gamma(x)=vxv^{*}\,(x\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
. Similarly, there is a unique unitary
\widehat{v}\colon\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})\rightarrow\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})
which implements
\widehat{\gamma}
and satisfies
J_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\widehat{v}=\widehat{v}J_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}
,
\widehat{v}\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}}=\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}
. Here
\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}
is the left Haar integral on
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}\subseteq\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})
is the associated positive cone
[
42
, Section 2.23]
, similarly for other quantum groups.

We begin with a general technical lemma of independent interest.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be a locally compact quantum group,
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}
and
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{K})
such that
(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}
. Then
\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}(\,(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\,)\rangle=\omega(x^{*})
.

Proof.

Define the subspace
\mathscr{J}=\{\nu\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{K})\mid\exists_{C>0}\forall_{y\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}}|\nu(y^{*})|\leq C\|\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}(y)\|\}
, and similarly its dual version
\widehat{\mathscr{J}}\subseteq\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{K}})
. By the construction of the dual Haar integral
[
27
, Page 75]
, we have that
\{(\nu\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\mid\nu\in\mathscr{J}\}
forms a
\sigma\textsc{-sot}^{*}\times\|\cdot\|
core for
\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}
and
\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}(z)|\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}((\nu\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}})\rangle=\nu(z^{*})
for
z\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}},\nu\in\mathscr{J}
. Consequently we can find nets
\omega_{i}\in\mathscr{J}\,(i\in I)
,
\widehat{\omega}_{j}\in\widehat{\mathscr{J}}(j\in J)
such that

(\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{\sigma\textsc{-sot}^{*}}(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}},\quad\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}(\,(\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\,)\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}(\,(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\,)

and

(\widehat{\omega}_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}\xrightarrow[j\in J]{\sigma\textsc{-sot}^{*}}x,\quad\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}(\,(\widehat{\omega}_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}\,)\xrightarrow[j\in J]{}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}(x).

Using this, we calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}(\,(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\,)\rangle=\lim_{j\in J}\lim_{i\in I}\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{K}}}((\widehat{\omega}_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}})\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}}}(\,(\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}}\,)\rangle\\
&=\lim_{j\in J}\lim_{i\in I}\omega_{i}\bigl(((\widehat{\omega}_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}})^{*}\bigr)=\lim_{j\in J}\lim_{i\in I}(\overline{\widehat{\omega}_{j}}\otimes\omega_{i})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}*}=\lim_{j\in J}\lim_{i\in I}\overline{\widehat{\omega}_{j}}((\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}})\\
&=\lim_{j\in J}\overline{\widehat{\omega}_{j}}((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}})=\lim_{j\in J}\overline{(\overline{\omega}\otimes\widehat{\omega}_{j})\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{K}*}}=\lim_{j\in J}\overline{\overline{\omega}(\,(\widehat{\omega}_{j}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{K}}})}=\overline{\overline{\omega}(x)}=\omega(x^{*}).\end{split}

∎

Proposition 9.2
.

We have
\widehat{v}=v^{*}
.

Proof.

Observe first that
\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}\circ\gamma
is a n.s.f. weight on
\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
and for
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{+},\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G})^{+}
we have, using the strong form of left invariance
[
27
, Proposition 3.1]

\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}\circ\gamma((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x))=\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}((\omega\gamma^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}(\gamma(x)))=\omega(\gamma^{-1}(\mathbbm{1}))\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}(\gamma(x))=\omega(\mathbbm{1})\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}(\gamma(x)),

which shows that
\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}\circ\gamma
is left invariant. Uniqueness of Haar integrals
[
50
, Theorem 3.5]
gives
t>0
such that
\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}\circ\gamma=t^{-2}\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}
. In particular
\gamma
restricts to a bijection
\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}
and
\gamma\circ\sigma^{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}_{s}=\sigma^{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}_{s}\circ\gamma\,(s\in\mathbb{R})
. Define

v_{0}\colon\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})\colon v_{0}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)=t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x))\quad(x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}).

One easily sees that
v_{0}
is well defined and extends to a unitary map. We claim that
v_{0}=v
. For
x_{1}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}
such that
x_{1}\in\Dom(\sigma^{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}_{i/2})
,
\sigma^{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}_{i/2}(x)^{*}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}
we have

J_{\mathbb{H}}v_{0}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x_{1})=t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\sigma^{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}_{i/2}(\gamma(x_{1}))^{*})=t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(\sigma^{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}_{i/2}(x_{1})^{*}))=v_{0}J_{\mathbb{G}}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x_{1}).

Density of the space of vectors
\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x_{1})

[
41
, Theorem 10.20]
shows
J_{\mathbb{H}}v_{0}=v_{0}J_{\mathbb{G}}
. For
x_{2}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),x_{3}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}
we have

\gamma(x_{2})t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x_{3}))=t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x_{2}x_{3}))=v_{0}x_{2}v^{*}_{0}v_{0}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x_{3})=v_{0}x_{2}v^{*}_{0}t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(x_{3})

which shows
\gamma(x_{2})=v_{0}x_{2}v_{0}^{*}
, i.e.
v_{0}
implements
\gamma
. Finally, by
[
41
, Equation (3), page 355]
we have

\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}=\overline{\{xJ_{\mathbb{G}}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\,|\,x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}\cap{\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}}^{*}\}}

and similarly for
\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}
. Consequently

\begin{split}&\quad\;v_{0}\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}=\overline{\{v_{0}xJ_{\mathbb{G}}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\,|\,x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}\cap{\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}}^{*}\}}=\overline{\{\gamma(x)J_{\mathbb{H}}v_{0}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\,|\,x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}\cap{\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}}^{*}\}}\\
&=\overline{\{\gamma(x)J_{\mathbb{H}}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x))\,|\,x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}\cap{\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}}^{*}\}}=\overline{\{yJ_{\mathbb{H}}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(y)\,|\,y\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}\cap{\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}}^{*}\}}=\mathfrak{P}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}.\end{split}

Uniqueness of the standard implementation
[
15
, Theorem 2.3]
allows us to conclude
v_{0}=v
. An analogous argument gives

(9.1)

\widehat{v}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}}(\widehat{y})=r\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}(\widehat{\gamma}(\widehat{y}))\quad(\widehat{y}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}})

for some
r>0
. Next, for
x_{3}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}},\omega_{1}\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{H})
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*})t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x_{3}))=t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}((\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}(\gamma(x_{3})))=t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma((\omega_{1}\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x_{3})))\\
&=v\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}((\omega_{1}\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x_{3}))=v(\omega_{1}\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}*})\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x_{3})=v\widehat{\gamma}\bigl((\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*})\bigr)v^{*}v\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x_{3})\\
&=v\widehat{\gamma}\bigl((\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*})\bigr)v^{*}t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x_{3})),\end{split}

hence
(\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*})=v\widehat{\gamma}\bigl((\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}*})\bigr)v^{*}
. A density argument shows that
v^{*}
implements
\widehat{\gamma}
.

Take
\widehat{x}\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}
and
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}
of the form
x=(\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
(see the previous lemma or
[
27
, Page 75]
). Using Lemma
9.1
we calculate
