\begin{split}&\quad\;\langle v\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}(\widehat{x})|v\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\rangle=\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}(\widehat{x})|\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\rangle=\widehat{\omega}(\widehat{x}^{*})=\widehat{\omega}\circ\widehat{\gamma}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x})^{*})=\bigl\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x}))\big|\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}\bigl((\widehat{\omega}\circ\widehat{\gamma}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\bigr)\bigr\rangle\\
&=\bigl\langle\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x}))\big|\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}\bigl(\gamma\bigl((\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr\rangle=\bigl\langle t^{-1}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x}))\big|t\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma(x))\bigr\rangle=\bigl\langle t^{-1}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x}))\big|v\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)\bigr\rangle.\end{split}

Density of the space of vectors of the form
\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)
shows
v\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}(\widehat{x})=t^{-1}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x}))
. Equation (
9.1
) gives
\Lambda_{\varphi_{\mathbb{H}}}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}(\widehat{x}))=r^{-1}\widehat{v}^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}}(\widehat{x})
, hence again by a density argument we find
v=t^{-1}r^{-1}\widehat{v}^{*}
. Taking norms gives
t=r^{-1}
and
v=\widehat{v}^{*}
as claimed.
∎

9.2.
Comment about left-right conventions

In our paper, we have decided to work with left actions. Right actions of locally compact quantum groups on von Neumann algebras are defined in a completely analogous way, as injective, unital normal
*
-homomorphisms
\M\rightarrow\M\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
satisfying the natural coaction condition. Working with left or right actions is a matter of choice. There are also ways of passing between them, hence typically one can translate results between these conventions. For example, if
\alpha^{\M}\colon\M\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}\M
is a left action, then
\chi\alpha\colon\M\rightarrow\M\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}^{op})
is a right action of
\mathbb{G}^{op}
on
\M
, and
(j_{\M}\otimes R_{\mathbb{G}})\chi\alpha^{\M}j_{\M}^{-1}\colon\M^{op}\rightarrow\M^{op}\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a right action of
\mathbb{G}
on
\M^{op}
(
j_{\M}\colon\M\rightarrow\M^{op}
is the canonical
*
-linear, antimultiplicative map which can be spatially realised as
j_{\M}=J_{\M}(\cdot)^{*}J_{\M}
when
\M\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\M))
and
J_{\M}
is the modular conjugation).

While left actions are implemented by (left) representations, right actions are implemented by right representations. More precisely, a (unitary) right representation of
\mathbb{G}
on
\mathsf{H}
is a unitary element
U\in\M(\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}_{U})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G}))
satisfying
(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})U=U_{[12]}U_{[13]}
. Any such
U
can be written as
U=(\phi_{U}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{V}^{\mathbb{G}}
for a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\phi_{U}\colon\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widecheck{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow\B(\mathsf{H}_{U})
(
\widecheck{\mathbb{G}}=\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
is the “right dual”). Then we say that
U
(or
\phi_{U}
) implements the right action
\alpha\colon\M\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
if
\M\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{U})
and
\alpha(m)=U(m\otimes\mathbbm{1})U^{*}\,(m\in\M)
.

Let us (partially) indicate how our results change when passing to right conventions. First, with a bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
we associate its right version
\widecheck{\mathcal{X}}=(j_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes j_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}})^{*}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widecheck{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widecheck{\mathbb{G}})
. It has the lift
\widecheck{\mathcal{X}}^{u}\in\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widecheck{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widecheck{\mathbb{G}}))
. Next, in the right setting, the definition of braided flip operators and braided tensor product change. Let
U=(\phi_{U}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{V}^{\mathbb{H}},V=(\phi_{V}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{V}^{\mathbb{G}}
be right representations, then we define

{}^{U}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{V}=(\phi_{V}\otimes\phi_{U})(\widecheck{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}\Sigma\colon\mathsf{H}_{U}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{V}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{V}\otimes\mathsf{H}_{U}.

If
\M\curvearrowleft\mathbb{H},\N\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
are right actions implemented by
U^{\M}=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{V}^{\mathbb{H}}
,
U^{\N}=(\phi_{\N}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathds{V}^{\mathbb{G}}
, then one defines

\M\overline{\boxtimes}\N=\overline{\lin}^{\sigma\textsc{-wot}}\,\{\iota_{\M}(m)\iota_{\N}(n)\mid m\in\M,n\in\N\}

where (writing
{}^{\N}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\M}={}^{U^{\N}}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{U^{\M}}
)

\iota_{\M}(m)=m\otimes\mathbbm{1}\;(m\in\M),\quad\iota_{\N}(n)={}^{\N}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\M}(n\otimes\mathbbm{1})({}^{\N}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\M})^{*}=(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widecheck{\mathcal{X}}^{u})^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes n)(\phi_{\M}\otimes\phi_{\N})(\widecheck{\mathcal{X}}^{u})\;(n\in\N).

10.
